### Q1. JEE Main 2026 (08 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Which of the following statements are not correct?
A. For water, magnitude of $K_b$ is more than the magnitude of $K_f$.
B. The elevation in boiling point of water when a non-volatile solute is added to it is larger in magnitude than its depression in freezing point.
C. Osmotic pressure measurement is preferred over any other colligative property to determine molar mass of proteins and polymers.
D. The dimerised form of benzoic acid in benzene is $C_6H_5-\underset{O}{\overset{O}{\|}}C-OH \cdots\cdots O=\underset{OH}{\overset{|}{C}}-C_6H_5$
Choose the correct answer from the options given below:

**Options:**

- **(A)** A and B only
- **(B)** A and D only
- **(C)** A, B and D only
- **(D)** A, C and D only

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) A, B and D only**

**Detailed Solution:**

For water, the molal elevation constant $K_b = 0.52 \text{ K kg mol}^{-1}$ and the molal depression constant $K_f = 1.86 \text{ K kg mol}^{-1}$. Thus, $K_b < K_f$. Statement A is incorrect.

For a solution with a given molality $m$ of a non-volatile solute, the elevation in boiling point is $\Delta T_b = K_b m$ and the depression in freezing point is $\Delta T_f = K_f m$. Since $K_b < K_f$, it follows that $\Delta T_b < \Delta T_f$. Statement B is incorrect.

Osmotic pressure measurement is preferred for determining the molar mass of macromolecules such as proteins and polymers because the measurement is done at room temperature and the magnitude of osmotic pressure is significant even for very dilute solutions. Statement C is correct.

In benzene, benzoic acid undergoes dimerization through intermolecular hydrogen bonding, forming a cyclic dimer with two hydrogen bonds. The structure given in statement D represents a linear association with only one hydrogen bond. Statement D is incorrect.

Therefore, statements A, B, and D are incorrect.

Answer: A, B and D only

</details>

### Q2. JEE Main 2026 (06 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Given below are two statements :
<img style="width: 250px" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main_2026_april_watermarked/06APRS2__q55_d1.jpg" />
Statement I : H$_2$O molecules move from the chamber $1$ to chamber $2$.
Statement II : The osmotic pressure of a solution prepared by dissolving $50$ mg of potassium sulphate (molar mass $= 174$ g/mol) in $2$ L of water (at $27°$C) is $0.0107$ bar. (Given : R $= 0.083$ dm$^3$ bar K$^{-1}$ mol$^{-1}$ and assume complete dissociation of electrolyte)
In the light of the above statements, choose the correct answer from the options given below :

**Options:**

- **(A)** Both Statement I and Statement II are true
- **(B)** Both Statement I and Statement II are false
- **(C)** Statement I is true but Statement II is false
- **(D)** Statement I is false but Statement II is true

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) Statement I is false but Statement II is true**

**Detailed Solution:**

For Statement I:
Molarity of the solution in Chamber 1 ($M_1$) = $\dfrac{18 / 180}{100 / 1000} = 1$ M
Molarity of the solution in Chamber 2 ($M_2$) = $\dfrac{30 / 180}{250 / 1000} = \dfrac{1/6}{1/4} = 0.667$ M
Since $M_1 > M_2$, the solution in Chamber 1 has a higher solute concentration. During osmosis, the solvent (water) moves from the region of lower solute concentration to the region of higher solute concentration across a semi-permeable membrane. Thus, water will move from Chamber 2 to Chamber 1. Statement I is false.

For Statement II:
The osmotic pressure is given by $\pi = iCRT$
For $K_2SO_4$, assuming complete dissociation ($K_2SO_4 \rightarrow 2K^+ + SO_4^{2-}$), the van't Hoff factor $i = 3$.
Concentration $C = \dfrac{50 \times 10^{-3}}{174 \times 2}$ mol/L
$\pi = 3 \times \left( \dfrac{50 \times 10^{-3}}{174 \times 2} \right) \times 0.083 \times 300$
$\pi = \dfrac{3 \times 0.05 \times 24.9}{348} = \dfrac{3.735}{348} \approx 0.0107$ bar
Statement II is true.

Therefore, Statement I is false but Statement II is true.

Answer: Statement I is false but Statement II is true

</details>

### Q3. JEE Main 2026 (05 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

$20\text{ g}$ hemoglobin in a $1\text{ L}$ aqueous solution (A) at $300\text{ K}$ is separated from pure water by semi permeable membrane. At equilibrium the height of solution in a tube dipped in a solution (A) is found to be $80.0\text{ mm}$ higher than the tube dipped in water. The molar mass of hemoglobin is _______ $\text{kg mol}^{-1}$. (Nearest integer)
(Given : $g = 10\text{ m s}^{-2}$, $R = 8.3\text{ kPa dm}^3\text{ K}^{-1}\text{mol}^{-1}$, density of solution $= 1000\text{ kg m}^{-3}$)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **62**

**Detailed Solution:**

The osmotic pressure $\pi$ of the solution is balanced by the hydrostatic pressure of the liquid column.

$\pi = \rho g h$

Given:
Density of solution, $\rho = 1000 \text{ kg m}^{-3}$
Acceleration due to gravity, $g = 10 \text{ m s}^{-2}$
Height difference, $h = 80.0 \text{ mm} = 0.08 \text{ m}$

$\pi = 1000 \times 10 \times 0.08 = 800 \text{ Pa} = 0.8 \text{ kPa}$

From the van 't Hoff equation for osmotic pressure:
$\pi = C R T = \dfrac{n}{V} R T$

Given:
Volume of solution, $V = 1 \text{ L} = 1 \text{ dm}^3$
Gas constant, $R = 8.3 \text{ kPa dm}^3 \text{ K}^{-1} \text{mol}^{-1}$
Temperature, $T = 300 \text{ K}$

Substituting the values:
$0.8 = \dfrac{n}{1} \times 8.3 \times 300$

$n = \dfrac{0.8}{2490} \text{ mol}$

The mass of hemoglobin is $w = 20 \text{ g} = 0.02 \text{ kg}$.
The molar mass $M$ in $\text{kg mol}^{-1}$ is:
$M = \dfrac{w}{n} = \dfrac{0.02}{\dfrac{0.8}{2490}}$

$M = \dfrac{0.02 \times 2490}{0.8} = \dfrac{49.8}{0.8} = 62.25 \text{ kg mol}^{-1}$

Rounding to the nearest integer, we get $62$.

Answer: $62$

</details>

### Q4. JEE Main 2026 (04 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

At $27°$C, $0.1$ M, $1$ L $K_4[Fe(CN)_6]$ aqueous solution and $0.1$ M, $1$ L $FeCl_3$ aqueous solution are placed in a container separated by a semi permeable membrane AB. Assume complete dissociation of both the solutes. Which of the following statement is correct?
<img style="width:350px;" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main_2026_april_watermarked/04APRS2__q54_d1.jpg" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** Blue color is formed on both sides.
- **(B)** Ionic solutes in aqueous solution can pass through semi-permeable membrane.
- **(C)** Solution on side '$y$' is hypotonic.
- **(D)** To cause the reverse flow of solvent during osmosis, external pressure (any value) should be applied to side '$x$'.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) Solution on side '$y$' is hypotonic.**

**Detailed Solution:**

For complete dissociation, the van 't Hoff factor $i$ is equal to the number of ions produced per formula unit.

For side 'x' containing $K_4[Fe(CN)_6]$:
$K_4[Fe(CN)_6] \rightarrow 4K^+ + [Fe(CN)_6]^{4-}$
Number of ions, $i_x = 5$
Effective concentration (osmolarity) $= i_x \times C = 5 \times 0.1 = 0.5$ M

For side 'y' containing $FeCl_3$:
$FeCl_3 \rightarrow Fe^{3+} + 3Cl^-$
Number of ions, $i_y = 4$
Effective concentration (osmolarity) $= i_y \times C = 4 \times 0.1 = 0.4$ M

Since the effective concentration of side 'y' ($0.4$ M) is less than that of side 'x' ($0.5$ M), the solution on side 'y' has a lower osmotic pressure and is therefore hypotonic with respect to side 'x'.

Evaluating the other options:
A semi-permeable membrane (SPM) only allows solvent molecules to pass, not solute ions. Thus, $Fe^{3+}$ and $[Fe(CN)_6]^{4-}$ cannot mix, and no blue color is formed.
To cause reverse osmosis, the external pressure applied to the hypertonic side (side 'x') must be strictly greater than the osmotic pressure difference ($\Delta \pi = \pi_x - \pi_y$), not just any value.

Answer: Solution on side '$y$' is hypotonic.

</details>

### Q5. JEE Main 2026 (02 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Solution A is prepared by dissolving $1$ g of a protein (molar mass $= 50000$ g mol$^{-1}$) in $0.5$ L of water at $300$ K. Its osmotic pressure is $x$ bar. Solution B is made by dissolving $2$ g of same protein in $1$ L of water at $300$ K. Osmotic pressure of solution B is $y$ bar. Entire solution of A is mixed with entire solution of B at same temperature. The osmotic pressure of resultant solution is $z$ bar. $x, y$ and $z$ respectively are:
$(R = 0.083$ L bar mol$^{-1}$ K$^{-1})$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $9.96 \times 10^{-4}; 9.96 \times 10^{-4}; 9.96 \times 10^{-4}$
- **(B)** $9.96 \times 10^{-4}; 9.96 \times 10^{-4}; 19.92 \times 10^{-4}$
- **(C)** $4.98 \times 10^{-4}; 4.98 \times 10^{-4}; 9.96 \times 10^{-4}$
- **(D)** $4.98 \times 10^{-4}; 4.98 \times 10^{-4}; 4.98 \times 10^{-4}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $9.96 \times 10^{-4}; 9.96 \times 10^{-4}; 9.96 \times 10^{-4}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Osmotic pressure is given by $\pi = CRT = \dfrac{w}{MV}RT$

For solution A:
$x = \dfrac{1}{50000 \times 0.5} \times 0.083 \times 300$
$x = \dfrac{1}{25000} \times 24.9 = 9.96 \times 10^{-4}$ bar

For solution B:
$y = \dfrac{2}{50000 \times 1} \times 0.083 \times 300$
$y = \dfrac{2}{50000} \times 24.9 = 9.96 \times 10^{-4}$ bar

For the resultant solution (mixture of A and B):
Total mass of protein $= 1 + 2 = 3$ g
Total volume of solution $= 0.5 + 1 = 1.5$ L
$z = \dfrac{3}{50000 \times 1.5} \times 0.083 \times 300$
$z = \dfrac{3}{75000} \times 24.9 = 9.96 \times 10^{-4}$ bar

Therefore, $x = y = z = 9.96 \times 10^{-4}$ bar.

Answer: $9.96 \times 10^{-4}; 9.96 \times 10^{-4}; 9.96 \times 10^{-4}$

</details>

### Q6. JEE Main 2026 (02 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

$19.5$ g of fluoro acetic acid (molar mass $= 78$ g mol$^{-1}$) is dissolved in $500$ g of water at $298$ K. The depression in the freezing point of water was $1°$C. What is $K_a$ of fluoro acetic acid? (For water, $K_f = 1.86$ K kg mol$^{-1}$). Assume molarity and molality to have same values.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $10^{-6}$
- **(B)** $4 \times 10^{-4}$
- **(C)** $3 \times 10^{-5}$
- **(D)** $3 \times 10^{-3}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $3 \times 10^{-3}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The molality of the solution is given by:

$m = \dfrac{\text{Mass of solute}}{\text{Molar mass of solute} \times \text{Mass of solvent in kg}}$

$m = \dfrac{19.5}{78 \times 0.5} = 0.5 \text{ mol kg}^{-1}$

The depression in freezing point is given by:

$\Delta T_f = i \times K_f \times m$

Substituting the given values:

$1 = i \times 1.86 \times 0.5$

$1 = i \times 0.93$

$i = \dfrac{1}{0.93} = \dfrac{100}{93}$

For the dissociation of a weak acid $HA \rightleftharpoons H^+ + A^-$, the van 't Hoff factor is $i = 1 + \alpha$, where $\alpha$ is the degree of dissociation.

$1 + \alpha = \dfrac{100}{93}$

$\alpha = \dfrac{100}{93} - 1 = \dfrac{7}{93}$

The acid dissociation constant $K_a$ is given by:

$K_a = \dfrac{C \alpha^2}{1 - \alpha}$

Since molarity and molality have the same values, $C = 0.5 \text{ M}$.

$K_a = \dfrac{0.5 \times \left(\dfrac{7}{93}\right)^2}{1 - \dfrac{7}{93}}$

$K_a = \dfrac{0.5 \times \dfrac{49}{93^2}}{\dfrac{86}{93}} = \dfrac{0.5 \times 49}{93 \times 86}$

$K_a = \dfrac{24.5}{7998} \approx 3.06 \times 10^{-3}$

The closest value is $3 \times 10^{-3}$.

Answer: $3 \times 10^{-3}$

</details>

### Q7. JEE Main 2026 (28 January Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Consider the following aqueous solutions. 
 I. 2.2 g Glucose in 125 mL of solution. 
 II. 1.9 g Calcium chloride in 250 mL of solution. 
 III. 9.0 g Urea in 500 mL of solution. 
 IV. 20.5 g Aluminium sulphate in 750 mL of solution.
The correct increasing order of boiling point of these solutions will be : 
 [Given : Molar mass in $\mathrm{g} \mathrm{mol}^{-1}: \mathrm{H}=1, \mathrm{C}=12, \mathrm{~N}=14, \mathrm{O}=16, \mathrm{Cl}=35.5, \mathrm{Ca}=40, \mathrm{Al}=27$ and $\mathrm{S}=32$]

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\text { III }<\text { I }<\text { II }<\text { IV }$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{I}<\mathrm{II}<\mathrm{III}<\mathrm{IV}$
- **(C)** $\text { II }<\text { III }<\text { I }<\text { IV }$
- **(D)** $\text { II }<\text { III }<\text { IV }<\text { I }$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\mathrm{I}<\mathrm{II}<\mathrm{III}<\mathrm{IV}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\Delta T_b = i \cdot k_b \cdot m$
For dilute solution ($M = m$)
<table border='1' style="width: auto; border-collapse: collapse;"><tr><th>Molarity</th><th>$i \times m$</th></tr><tr><td>(I) $M_{glucose} = \dfrac{2.2}{180} \times \dfrac{1000}{125} = 0.098$</td><td>$0.098 \times 1$</td></tr><tr><td>(II) $M_{CaCl_2} = \dfrac{1.9}{111} \times \dfrac{1000}{250} = 0.068$</td><td>$0.068 \times 3$</td></tr><tr><td>(III) $M_{urea} = \dfrac{9}{60} \times \dfrac{1000}{500} = 0.3$</td><td>$0.3 \times 1$</td></tr><tr><td>(IV) $M_{Al_2(SO_4)_2} = \dfrac{20.5}{342} \times \dfrac{1000}{750} \approx 0.08$</td><td>$0.08 \times 5$</td></tr></table>
Order of $\Delta T_b$ = $Al_2(SO_4)_3$ > Urea > $CaCl_2$ > Glucose
So order of BP = $Al_2(SO_4)_3$ > Urea > $CaCl_2$ > Glucose
So Answer will be I < II < III < IV

</details>

### Q8. JEE Main 2026 (24 January Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

' W ' g of a non-volatile electrolyte solid solute of molar mass ' M ' $\mathrm{g} \mathrm{mol}^{-1}$ when dissolved in 100 mL water, decreases vapour pressure of water from 640 mm Hg to 600 mm Hg. If aqueous solution of the electrolyte boils at 375 K and $\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{b}}$ for water is $0.52 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$, then the mole fraction of the electrolyte solute $\left(x_{2}\right)$ in the solution can be expressed as 
 (Given : density of water $=1 \mathrm{~g} / \mathrm{mL}$ and boiling point of water $=373 \mathrm{~K}$)

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{2 \cdot 6}{16} \times \frac{\mathrm{M}}{\mathrm{W}}$
- **(B)** $\frac{16}{2 \cdot 6} \times \frac{\mathrm{W}}{\mathrm{M}}$
- **(C)** $\frac{1.3}{8} \times \frac{\mathrm{M}}{\mathrm{W}}$
- **(D)** $\frac{1 \cdot 3}{8} \times \frac{\mathrm{W}}{\mathrm{M}}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\frac{1 \cdot 3}{8} \times \frac{\mathrm{W}}{\mathrm{M}}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given the vapour pressure of pure water $P^o = 640$ mm Hg and vapour pressure of solution $P_s = 600$ mm Hg.
According to Raoult's law for an electrolyte solution, the relative lowering of vapour pressure is given by $\frac{P^o - P_s}{P^o} = i \cdot x_2$, where $i$ is the van't Hoff factor and $x_2$ is the mole fraction of the solute.
Substituting the values: $\frac{640 - 600}{640} = i \cdot x_2$
$\frac{40}{640} = i \cdot x_2 \implies i \cdot x_2 = \frac{1}{16}$ ... (1)
The elevation in boiling point is given by $\Delta T_b = i \cdot K_b \cdot m$, where $m$ is the molality.
Given $T_b = 375$ K and $T_b^o = 373$ K, so $\Delta T_b = 375 - 373 = 2$ K.
Molality $m = \frac{W}{M \times 100} \times 1000 = \frac{10W}{M}$ (since mass of water = $100$ mL $\times 1$ g/mL = $100$ g).
Substituting in the elevation formula: $2 = i \cdot 0.52 \cdot \frac{10W}{M}$
$i = \frac{2M}{5.2W} = \frac{20M}{52W} = \frac{5M}{13W}$ ... (2)
Substitute the value of $i$ from (2) into (1):
$\frac{5M}{13W} \cdot x_2 = \frac{1}{16}$
$x_2 = \frac{1}{16} \cdot \frac{13W}{5M} = \frac{13}{80} \cdot \frac{W}{M}$
This can be rewritten as $x_2 = \frac{1.3}{8} \times \frac{W}{M}$.

</details>

### Q9. JEE Main 2026 (24 January Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

A solution is prepared by dissolving 0.3 g of a non-volatile non-electrolyte solute 'A' of molar mass $60 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$ and 0.9 g of a non-volatile non-electrolyte solute ' $B$ ' of molar mass $180 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$ in $100 \mathrm{~mL} \mathrm{H}_{2} \mathrm{O}$ at $27^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$. Osmotic pressure of the solution will be 
 [Given: $\mathrm{R}=0.082 \mathrm{~L} \mathrm{~atm} \mathrm{~K}^{-1} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$]

**Options:**

- **(A)** 0.82 atm
- **(B)** 2.46 atm
- **(C)** 1.23 atm
- **(D)** 1.47 atm

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) 2.46 atm**

**Detailed Solution:**

Calculate osmotic pressure using $\pi = \frac{nRT}{V}$ where n is total moles of solute particles. 
Moles of solute A: $n_A = \frac{0.3 \text{ g}}{60 \text{ g/mol}} = 0.005$ mol 
Moles of solute B: $n_B = \frac{0.9 \text{ g}}{180 \text{ g/mol}} = 0.005$ mol 
Total moles: $n_{total} = 0.005 + 0.005 = 0.01$ mol 
Volume of solution: 100 mL = 0.1 L 
Temperature: 27°C = 300 K 
Osmotic pressure: $\pi = \frac{0.01 \times 0.082 \times 300}{0.1} = \frac{0.246}{0.1} = 2.46$ atm

</details>

---

### Q10. JEE Main 2026 (21 January Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The osmotic pressure of a living cell is 12 atm at 300 K. The strength of sodium chloride solution that is isotonic with the living cell at this temperature is $\_\_\_\_$ $\mathrm{g} \mathrm{L}^{-1}$. (Nearest integer) 
 Given : $\mathrm{R}=0.08 \mathrm{~L} \mathrm{~atm} \mathrm{~K}^{-1} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$ 
 Assume complete dissociation of NaCl  
 (Given : Molar mass of Na and Cl are 23 and $35.5 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$ respectively.)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **15**

**Detailed Solution:**

For osmotic pressure, using the van 't Hoff equation: $\pi = iMRT$.
The living cell has osmotic pressure of 12 atm.
For an isotonic NaCl solution, the osmotic pressures must be equal. 
For NaCl solution: $\pi = i \times M \times R \times T$, where $i = 2$ (complete dissociation into Na⁺ and Cl⁻). 
$12 = 2 \times M \times 0.08 \times 300$ 
$12 = 48M$ 
$M = 0.25$ mol/L 
Molar mass of NaCl = 23 + 35.5 = 58.5 g/mol. 
Concentration in g/L = $0.25 \times 58.5 = 14.625 \approx 15$ g/L

</details>

---

### Q11. JEE Main 2026 (21 January Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

A substance ' X ' (1.5 g) dissolved in 150 g of a solvent ' Y ' (molar mass $=300 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$) led to an elevation of the boiling point by 0.5 K. The relative lowering in the vapour pressure of the solvent ' Y ' is $\_\_\_\_$ $\times 10^{-2}$. (nearest integer) 
 [Given : $\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{b}}$ of the solvent $=5.0 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$ ] 
 Assume the solution to be dilute and no association or dissociation of $X$ takes place in solution.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **3**

**Detailed Solution:**

Using boiling point elevation to find molality:
$\Delta T_b = K_b \times m$ gives $0.5 = 5.0 \times m$, so $m = 0.1$ mol/kg.
Converting molality to moles of solute:
$0.1 = \frac{\text{moles of X}}{0.15}$, giving moles of X = 0.015 mol.
Molar mass of X = 1.5 g / 0.015 mol = 100 g/mol.
For vapor pressure lowering, the relative lowering equals the mole fraction of solute.
Moles of solvent Y = 150 g / 300 g/mol = 0.5 mol.
Relative lowering of vapor pressure
= $\frac{n_X}{n_X + n_Y} = \frac{0.015}{0.015 + 0.5}$ 
$= \frac{0.015}{0.515} = 0.0291 \approx 3 \times 10^{-2}$

</details>

### Q12. JEE Main 2026 (21 January Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Elements P and Q form two types of non-volatile, non-ionizable compounds PQ and $\mathrm{PQ}_{2}$. When 1 g of PQ is dissolved in 50 g of solvent ${ }^{\prime} \mathrm{A}^{\prime}, \Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{b}}$ was 1.176 K while when 1 g of $\mathrm{PQ}_{2}$ is dissolved in 50 g of solvent ${ }^{\prime} \mathrm{A}^{\prime}, \Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{b}}$ was 0.689 K. ($\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{b}}$ of ' $\mathrm{A}^{\prime}=5 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$). The molar masses of elements P and Q (in $\mathrm{g} \mathrm{mol}^{-1}$) respectively, are :

**Options:**

- **(A)** $70,110$
- **(B)** $65,145$
- **(C)** $25,60$
- **(D)** $60,25$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $25,60$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Using $\Delta T_b = K_b \times \frac{w}{M} \times \frac{1000}{W}$
For PQ: $1.176 = 5 \times \frac{1}{M_{PQ}} \times \frac{1000}{50} = \frac{100}{M_{PQ}}$
$M_{PQ} = \frac{100}{1.176} = 85$ g/mol
For $PQ_2$: $0.689 = \frac{100}{M_{PQ_2}}$
$M_{PQ_2} = \frac{100}{0.689} = 145$ g/mol
$P + Q = 85$ ... (i)
$P + 2Q = 145$ ... (ii)
Subtracting: $Q = 60$ g/mol
From (i): $P = 85 - 60 = 25$ g/mol

</details>

---

### Q13. JEE Main 2025 (8 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/sPP3V6miwtiOF1MnUV-9sMmJgqT_ufRN-bnVZM6KX8k.original.fullsize.png"/>

**Options:**

- **(A)** $1.38 \times 10^{-3}$
- **(B)** $1.1 \times 10^{-2}$
- **(C)** $1.90 \times 10^{-3}$
- **(D)** $1.89 \times 10^{-1}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $1.38 \times 10^{-3}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{f}}=\mathrm{ik}_{\mathrm{f}} \mathrm{m} \\ & 0.2=\mathrm{i} \times 1.8 \times 0.1 \\ & \mathrm{i}=\frac{20}{18}=\frac{10}{9} \\ & \text { For } \mathrm{HA}_{(\mathrm{aq})} \rightleftharpoons \mathrm{H}_{(\mathrm{aq})}^{+}+\mathrm{A}_{(\mathrm{aq})}^{-} \\ & \mathrm{t}=0 \quad 1 \\ & \mathrm{t}=\mathrm{t}_{\mathrm{eq}} 1-\alpha \quad \alpha \\ & \mathrm{i}=1+\alpha \\ & \frac{10}{9}=1+\alpha \\ & \alpha=\frac{1}{9} \\ & \mathrm{~K}_{\mathrm{eq}}=\frac{\left[\mathrm{H}^{+}\right]\left[\mathrm{A}^{-}\right]}{[\mathrm{HA}]}=\frac{\mathrm{C} \alpha^2}{1-\alpha} \\ & 0.1\left(\frac{1}{9}\right)^2 \\ & =\frac{1}{1-\frac{1}{9}}=\frac{1}{720} \\ & \mathrm{~K}_{\mathrm{eq}}=1.38 \times 10^{-3}\end{aligned}$

</details>

The percentage dissociation of a salt $\left(\mathrm{MX}_3\right)$ solution at given temperature (van't Hoff factor $\mathrm{i}=$ 2) is ______ % (Nearest integer)

**Correct Value:** **33**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\mathrm{MX}_3 \rightarrow \mathrm{M}^{+3}+3 \mathrm{X}^{\ominus}$
$\begin{aligned} & \mathrm{i}=1+(\mathrm{n}-1) \alpha \\ & \mathrm{i}=1+(4-1) \alpha=2\end{aligned}$
$\alpha=\frac{1}{3}=33.33 \% \approx 33 \%$

</details>

### Q15. JEE Main 2025 (4 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Given below are two statements :
Statement (I) : Molal depression constant $\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{f}}$ is given by $\frac{M_1 R T_f}{\Delta S_{f u s}}$, where symbols have their usual meaning.
Statement (II) : $\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{f}}$ for benzene is less than the $\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{f}}$ for water.
In the light of the above statements, choose the most appropriate answer from the options given below :

**Options:**

- **(A)** Statement I is incorrect but Statement II is correct
- **(B)** Both Statement I and Statement II are incorrect
- **(C)** Both Statement I and Statement II are correct
- **(D)** Statement I is correct but Statement II is incorrect

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) Statement I is correct but Statement II is incorrect**

**Detailed Solution:**

Statement-I
Molar depression constant $\mathrm{k}_f=\frac{\mathrm{M}_1 \mathrm{RT}_{\mathrm{f}}^2}{\Delta \mathrm{H}_{\text {fus }}}$
$\begin{aligned}
& \mathrm{k}_f=\frac{\mathrm{M}_1 \mathrm{RT}_{\mathrm{f}}}{\left[\frac{\Delta \mathrm{H}_{\text {fus }}}{\mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{f}}}\right]} \\
& \mathrm{k}_f=\frac{\mathrm{M}_1 \mathrm{RT}_f}{\Delta \mathrm{~S}_{\text {fus }}}
\end{aligned}$
Hence statement-I is correct
$\text { but } \mathrm{k}_{\mathrm{f}} \text { for benzene }=5.12 \frac{{ }^{\circ} \mathrm{C}}{\mathrm{molal}}$
$\mathrm{k}_{\mathrm{f}}$ for water $=1.86 \frac{{ }^{\circ} \mathrm{C}}{\text { molal }}$ Hence statement- II is incorrect

</details>

### Q17. JEE Main 2025 (3 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

2 moles each of ethylene glycol and glucose are dissolved in 500 g of water. The boiling point of the resulting solution is :
(Given : Ebullioscopic constant of water $\left.=0.52 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}\right)$

**Options:**

- **(A)** 379.2 K
- **(B)** 377.3 K
- **(C)** 375.3 K
- **(D)** 277.3 K

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) 377.3 K**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{b}}=\mathrm{i}_1 \mathrm{~m}_1 \mathrm{k}_{\mathrm{b}}+\mathrm{i}_2 \mathrm{~m}_2 \mathrm{k}_{\mathrm{b}} \\ & =1 \times \frac{2}{0.5} \times 0.52+\frac{1 \times 2}{0.5} \times 0.52=4.16 \\ & \left(\mathrm{~T}_{\mathrm{b}}\right)_{\text {solution }}=373.16+4.16=377.3 \mathrm{~K}.\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q18. JEE Main 2025 (2 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

When 1 g each of compounds AB and $\mathrm{AB}_2$ are dissolved in 15 g of water separately, they increased the boiling point of water by 2.7 K and 1.5 K respectively. The atomic mass of A (in amu) is ____ $\times 10^{-1}$ (Nearest integer)
(Given : Molal boiling point elevation constant is $\left.0.5 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}\right)$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **25**

**Detailed Solution:**

For AB
$\begin{aligned}
& \Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{b}}=2.7 \mathrm{~K} \\
& 2.7=1 \times 0.5 \times \mathrm{m} \\
& \mathrm{~m}=\frac{27}{5}
\end{aligned}$
Let molar mass of $\mathrm{AB}=\mathrm{x}$.
$\begin{aligned}
& \text { So } \frac{1 / x}{15} \times 1000=\frac{27}{5} \\
& x=12.34
\end{aligned}$
For $\mathrm{AB}_2$
$\begin{aligned}
& \Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{b}}=1.5 \mathrm{~K} \\
& 1.5=1 \times 0.5 \times \mathrm{m} \\
& \mathrm{~m}=3
\end{aligned}$
Let molar mass of $\mathrm{AB}_2=\mathrm{y}$
So $\frac{1 / \mathrm{y}}{15} \times 1000=3$
$\begin{aligned}
& y=\frac{1000}{45} \\
& y=22.22
\end{aligned}$
Now let a and b be atomic masses of A and B respectively, then
$\begin{aligned}
& \mathrm{A}+\mathrm{b}=12.34 \quad...(i)\\
& \mathrm{~A}+2 \mathrm{~b}=22.22 \quad...(ii) \\
& \mathrm{~B}=22.22-12.34=9.88 \\
& \text { Now } \mathrm{a}=12.34-9.88=2.46 \\
& =24.6 \times 10^{-1}=25 \times 10^{-1}
\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q21. JEE Main 2025 (29 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

1.24 g of \(\mathrm{AX}_2\) (molar mass \(124 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}\) ) is dissolved in 1 kg of water to form a solution with boiling point of \(100.0156^{\circ} \mathrm{C}\), while \(25.4 \mathrm{~g}^{\circ}\) of \(\mathrm{AY}_2\) (molar mass \(250 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}\) ) in 2 kg of water constitutes a solution with a boiling point of \(100.0260^{\circ} \mathrm{C}\).
\(\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{b}}\left(\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}\right)=0.52 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}\)
Which of the following is correct ?

**Options:**

- **(A)** \(\mathrm{AX}_2\) is fully ionised while \(\mathrm{AY}_2\) is completely unionised.
- **(B)** \(\mathrm{AX}_2\) is completely unionised while \(\mathrm{AY}_2\) is fully ionised.
- **(C)** \(\mathrm{AX}_2\) and \(\mathrm{AY}_2\) (both) are completely unionised.
- **(D)** \(\mathrm{AX}_2\) and \(\mathrm{AY}_2\) (both) are fully ionised.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) \(\mathrm{AX}_2\) is fully ionised while \(\mathrm{AY}_2\) is completely unionised.**

**Detailed Solution:**

For $\mathrm{AX}_2$
$\begin{aligned}
& \Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{b}}=\mathrm{i} \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~m} \\
& 0.0156=\mathrm{i} \times 0.52 \times \frac{1.24}{124 \times 1} \\
& 3=\mathrm{i} \\
& 3=1+2 \alpha \\
& 1=\alpha
\end{aligned}$

For $\mathrm{AY}_2$
$\begin{aligned}
& \Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{b}}=\mathrm{i} \mathrm{~K}_{\mathrm{b}} \mathrm{~m} \\
& 0.0260=\mathrm{i} \times 0.52 \times \frac{25.4}{250 \times 2} \\
& \mathrm{i} \approx 1
\end{aligned}$
$\therefore A X_2$ is completely ionised $\& A Y_2$ is completely unionised

</details>

### Q22. JEE Main 2025 (28 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

What is the freezing point depression constant of a solvent, 50 g of which contain 1 g non volatile solute (molar mass $256 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$ ) and the decrease in freezing point is 0.40 K ?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $3.72 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(B)** $1.86 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(C)** $4.43 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(D)** $5.12 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $5.12 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} \Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{f}} & =\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{b}} \cdot \mathrm{m} \\ 0.4 & =\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{b}} \frac{\frac{1}{256}}{50 \times 10^{-3}} \\ \mathrm{~K}_{\mathrm{b}} & =5.12 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} / \mathrm{mol}\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q23. JEE Main 2025 (24 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The observed and normal molar masses of compound $\mathrm{MX}_2$ are 65.6 and 164 respectively. The percent degree of ionisation of $\mathrm{MX}_2$ is $\ldots\ldots$ %. (Nearest integer)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **75**

**Detailed Solution:**

Normal molar mass of $\mathrm{MX}_2=164.0 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
Observed molar mass of $\mathrm{MX}_2=65.6 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
$\begin{aligned}
& \text { Van't Hoff factor }(\mathrm{i})=\frac{\text { Normal molar mass }}{\text { Observed molar mass }} \\
& =\frac{164}{65.6}=2.5
\end{aligned}$
If $\alpha$ is the degree of ionisation, then
$\begin{aligned}
& \underset{1-\alpha}{\mathrm{MX}_2} \rightleftharpoons \mathrm{M}_\alpha^{2+}+\underset{2 \alpha}{2 \mathrm{X}^{-}} \\
& \mathrm{i}=1-\alpha+\alpha+2 \alpha=1+2 \alpha \\
& 1+2 \alpha=2.5 \\
& \alpha=0.75
\end{aligned}$
$\therefore$ Percent degree of ionisation $=75 \%$

</details>

### Q25. JEE Main 2025 (22 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

Arrange the following solutions in order of their increasing boiling points.
(i) $10^{-4} \mathrm{M~} \mathrm{NaCl}$
(ii) $10^{-4} \mathrm{M~}$ Urea
(iii) $10^{-3} \mathrm{M~} \mathrm{NaCl}$
(iv) $10^{-2} \mathrm{M~} \mathrm{NaCl}$

**Options:**

- **(A)** (i)  &lt; (ii) &lt; (iii) &lt; (iv)
- **(B)** (iv)  &lt; (iii) &lt; (i) &lt; (ii)
- **(C)** (ii)  &lt; (i$) \equiv($iii) &lt; (iv)
- **(D)** (ii)  &lt; (i) &lt; (iii) &lt; (iv)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) (ii)  &lt; (i) &lt; (iii) &lt; (iv)**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{b}}=\mathrm{i} \mathrm{~K}_{\mathrm{b}} . \mathrm{m} . \propto \mathrm{i} . \mathrm{C} .$
where $\mathrm{C}=$ concentration
$\begin{array}{|l|c|}
\hline \text { Options } & \text { i.C. } \\
\hline \text { (i) } & 2 \times 10^{-4} \\
\hline \text { (ii) } & 1 \times 10^{-4} \\
\hline \text { (iii) } & 2 \times 10^{-3} \\
\hline \text { (iv) } & 2 \times 10^{-2} \\
\hline
\end{array}$



B.P. order :
$\text { (ii) } < \text { (i) } < \text { (iii) } < \text { (iv) }$

</details>

### Q26. JEE Main 2024 (08 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

A solution containing \(10 \mathrm{~g}\) of an electrolyte \(\mathrm{AB}_2\) in \(100 \mathrm{~g}\) of water boils at \(100.52^{\circ} \mathrm{C}\). The degree of ionization of the electrolyte \((\alpha)\) is _______ \(\times 10^{-1}\). (nearest integer)
[Given : Molar mass of \(\mathrm{AB}_2=200 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}, \mathrm{~K}_{\mathrm{b}}\) (molal boiling point elevation const. of water) \(=0.52 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}\), boiling point of water \(=100^{\circ} \mathrm{C} ; \mathrm{AB}_2\) ionises as \(\left.\mathrm{AB}_2 \rightarrow \mathrm{A}^{2+}+2 \mathrm{~B}^{-}\right]\)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **5**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \mathrm{AB}_2 \rightarrow \mathrm{A}^{+2}+2 \mathrm{~B}^{\ominus} \\ & \mathrm{i}=1+(3-1) \alpha \\ & \mathrm{i}=1+2 \alpha \\ & \Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{b}}=\mathrm{k}_{\mathrm{b}} \mathrm{im} \\ & 0.52=0.52(1+2 \alpha) \frac{\frac{10}{200}}{\frac{100}{1000}} \\ & 1=(1+2 \alpha) \frac{10}{20} \\ & 2=1+2 \alpha \\ & \alpha=0.5\end{aligned}$
Ans. $\alpha=5 \times 10^{-1}$

</details>

### Q27. JEE Main 2024 (06 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

When ' \(x\) ' \(\times 10^{-2} \mathrm{~mL}\) methanol (molar mass \(=32 \mathrm{~g}\); density \(=0.792 \mathrm{~g} / \mathrm{cm}^3\)) is added to \(100 \mathrm{~mL}\) water (density \(=1 \mathrm{~g} / \mathrm{cm}^3\)), the following diagram is obtained.
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/v9ytlFh5QJQgBFhHmFvamzJVDPkONwyrqUL9ghK4RH4.original.fullsize.png">
\(x=\) ______ (nearest integer).
[Given : Molal freezing point depression constant of water at \(273.15 \mathrm{~K}\) is \(1.86 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}\)]

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **543**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{f}}=273.15-270.65=2.5 \mathrm{~K} \\ & \Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{f}}=\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{f}} \mathrm{m} \Rightarrow 2.5=1.86 \times \frac{\mathrm{n}}{0.1} \\ & \Rightarrow \mathrm{n}=0.1344 \text { moles } \\ & \Rightarrow \mathrm{w}=0.1344 \times 32=4.3 \mathrm{~g} \\ & \text { Volume }=\frac{4.3}{0.792}=5.43 \mathrm{ml}=543 \times 10^{-2} \mathrm{ml}\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q28. JEE Main 2024 (06 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

Consider the dissociation of the weak acid \(\mathrm{HX}\) as given below
\(\mathrm{HX}(\mathrm{aq}) \rightleftharpoons \mathrm{H}^{+}(\mathrm{aq})+\mathrm{X}^{-}(\mathrm{aq}), \mathrm{Ka}=1.2 \times 10^{-5}\)
\(\left[\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{a}}\right.\) : dissociation constant \(]\)
The osmotic pressure of \(0.03 \mathrm{M}\) aqueous solution of \(\mathrm{HX}\) at \(300 \mathrm{~K}\) is _______ \(\times 10^{-2}\) bar (nearest integer).
[Given : \(\mathrm{R}=0.083 \mathrm{~L} \mathrm{bar} \mathrm{mol}^{-1} \mathrm{~K}^{-1}\)]

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **76**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned}
& \mathrm{HX} \rightleftharpoons \mathrm{H}^{+}+\mathrm{X}^{-} \quad \mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{a}}=1.2 \times 10^{-5} \\
& 0.03 \mathrm{M} \\
& 0.03-\mathrm{x} \quad \mathrm{x} \quad \mathrm{x} \\
& \mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{a}}=1.2 \times 10^{-5}=\frac{\mathrm{x}^2}{0.03-\mathrm{x}} \\
& 0.03-\mathrm{x} \approx 0.03\left(\mathrm{~K}_{\mathrm{a}} \text { is very small }\right) \\
& \frac{\mathrm{x}^2}{0.03}=1.2 \times 10^{-5} \\
& \mathrm{x}=6 \times 10^{-4}
\end{aligned}$
Final solution : $0.03-x+x+x$
$\begin{aligned}
& =0.03+x=0.03+6 \times 10^{-4} \\
& \Pi=\left(0.03+\left(6 \times 10^{-4}\right)\right) \times 0.083 \times 300 \\
& =76.19 \times 10^{-2} \approx 76 \times 10^{-2}
\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q30. JEE Main 2024 (05 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Considering acetic acid dissociates in water, its dissociation constant is \(6.25 \times 10^{-5}\). If \(5 \mathrm{~mL}\) of acetic acid is dissolved in 1 litre water, the solution will freeze at \(-x \times 10^{-2}{ }^{\circ} \mathrm{C}\), provided pure water freezes at \(0^{\circ} \mathrm{C}\).
\(x=\) ______ . (Nearest integer)
Given :
\(\begin{aligned}
& \left(\mathrm{K}_f\right)_{\text {water }}=1.86 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1} \text {. } \\
& \text { density of acetic acid is } 1.2 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1} \text {. } \\
& \text { molar mass of water }=18 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1} \text {. } \\
& \text { molar mass of acetic acid=60 } \mathrm{g} \mathrm{mol}^{-1} \text {. } \\
& \text { density of water }=1 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~cm}^{-3}
\end{aligned}\)
Acetic acid dissociates as \(\mathrm{CH}_3 \mathrm{COOH} \rightleftharpoons \mathrm{CH}_3 \mathrm{COO}^{\ominus}+\mathrm{H}^{\oplus}\)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **19**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \text { Mass of } \mathrm{CH}_3 \mathrm{COOH}=\mathrm{V} \times \mathrm{d} \\ & =5 \mathrm{ml} \times 1.2 \mathrm{~g} / \mathrm{ml} \\ & =6 \mathrm{gm} \\ & \mathrm{n}_{\mathrm{CH}_3 \mathrm{COOH}}=\frac{6}{60}=0.1 \mathrm{~mol} \\ & \mathrm{~m}_{\mathrm{CH}_3 \mathrm{COOH}} \approx \mathrm{M}_{\mathrm{CH}_3 \mathrm{COOH}}=\frac{0.1}{1}=0.1 \mathrm{M} \\ & \mathrm{CH}_3 \mathrm{COOH} \rightleftharpoons \mathrm{CH}_3 \mathrm{COO}^{-}+\mathrm{H}^{+}\end{aligned}$
C
$\begin{aligned} & \mathrm{C}-\mathrm{C} \alpha \quad \mathrm{C} \alpha \quad \mathrm{C} \alpha \\ & \mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{a}}=\frac{\mathrm{C} \alpha^2}{1-\alpha} \\ & 1-\alpha \approx 1 \Rightarrow \mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{a}}=\mathrm{C} \alpha^2 \\ & \alpha=\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{Ka}}{\mathrm{C}}}=\sqrt{\frac{6.25 \times 10^{-5}}{0.1}}=25 \times 10^{-3}\end{aligned}$
$\begin{aligned} & \text { V.f. }(\mathrm{i})=1+\alpha(\mathrm{n}-1)=1+\alpha(2-1)=1+\alpha \\ & =1+25 \times 10^{-3}=1.025 \\ & \Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{f}}=\mathrm{i} \mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{f}} \mathrm{m} \\ & =(1.025)(1.86)(0.1) \\ & =0.19 \\ & =19 \times 10^{-2}\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q31. JEE Main 2024 (05 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

An artificial cell is made by encapsulating \(0.2 \mathrm{M}\) glucose solution within a semipermeable membrane. The osmotic pressure developed when the artificial cell is placed within a \(0.05 \mathrm{M}\) solution of \(\mathrm{NaCl}\) at \(300 \mathrm{~K}\) is _______ \(\times 10^{-1}\) bar. (nearest integer).
[Given : \(\mathrm{R}=0.083 \mathrm{~L} \mathrm{bar} \mathrm{mol}^{-1} \mathrm{~K}^{-1}\)]
Assume complete dissociation of \(\mathrm{NaCl}\)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **25**

**Detailed Solution:**

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/7jCpd9cJFAK2eDmIJ4eeI1L96AdET2Qrt4N3Hn0R8q0.original.fullsize.png">
$\begin{aligned} & \mathrm{NaCl} \longrightarrow \mathrm{Na}^{+}+\mathrm{Cl}^{-} \\ & 0.05 \mathrm{M} \quad 0.05 \mathrm{M} \quad 0.05 \mathrm{M} \\ & \text { Total } \mathrm{C}_1=0.05+0.05=0.1 \mathrm{M}(\mathrm{NaCl}) \\ & \mathrm{C}_2=0.2 \mathrm{M}(\text { glucose }) \\ & \pi=\left(\mathrm{C}_2-\mathrm{C}_1\right) \mathrm{RT} \\ & =(0.2-0.1) \times 0.083 \times 300 \\ & =2.49 \mathrm{bar} \\ & =24.9 \times 10^{-1} \text { bar }\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q32. JEE Main 2024 (04 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

\(2.7 \mathrm{~kg}\) of each of water and acetic acid are mixed. The freezing point of the solution will be \(-x^{\circ} \mathrm{C}\). Consider the acetic acid does not dimerise in water, nor dissociates in water. \(x=\) ______ (nearest integer)
[Given: Molar mass of water \(=18 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}\), acetic acid \(=60 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}\)
\(\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{f}} \mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}: 1.86 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}\)
\(\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{f}}\) acetic acid: \(3.90 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}\)
freezing point: \(\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}=273 \mathrm{~K}\), acetic acid \(=290 \mathrm{~K}\)]

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **31**

**Detailed Solution:**

As moles of water $>$ moles of $\mathrm{CH}_3 \mathrm{COOH}$ water is solvent.
$\begin{aligned}
& \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{F}}^{\circ}-\left(\mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{F}}\right)_{\mathrm{S}}=\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{F}} \times \mathrm{M} \\
& 0-\left(\mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{F}}\right)_{\mathrm{S}}=1.86 \times \frac{2700 / 60}{2700 / 1000} \\
& \left(\mathrm{~T}_{\mathrm{F}}\right)_{\mathrm{S}}=-31^{\circ} \mathrm{C} .
\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q33. JEE Main 2024 (04 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

\(2.5 \mathrm{~g}\) of a non-volatile, non-electrolyte is dissolved in \(100 \mathrm{~g}\) of water at \(25^{\circ} \mathrm{C}\). The solution showed a boiling point elevation by \(2^{\circ} \mathrm{C}\). Assuming the solute concentration is negligible with respect to the solvent concentration, the vapor pressure of the resulting aqueous solution is \(\qquad\) \(\mathrm{mm}\) of \(\mathrm{Hg}\) (nearest integer)
[Given : Molal boiling point elevation constant of water \(\left(\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{b}}\right)=0.52 \mathrm{~K} . \mathrm{kg} \mathrm{mol}^{-1}\),
\(1 \mathrm{~atm}\) pressure \(=760 \mathrm{~mm}\) of \(\mathrm{Hg}\), molar mass of water \(=18 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}\)]

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **707**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned}
& 2=0.52 \times \mathrm{m} \\
& \mathrm{m}=\frac{2}{0.52}
\end{aligned}$
According to question, solution is much diluted
$\begin{aligned}
& \text { so } \frac{\Delta \mathrm{P}}{\mathrm{P}^{\circ}}=\frac{\mathrm{n}_{\text {solute }}}{\mathrm{n}_{\text {solvent }}} \\
& \frac{\Delta \mathrm{P}}{\mathrm{P}^{\circ}}=\frac{\mathrm{m}}{1000} \times \mathrm{M}_{\text {solvent }} \\
& \Delta \mathrm{P}=\mathrm{P}^{\circ} \times \frac{\mathrm{m}}{1000} \times \mathrm{M}_{\text {solvent }} \\
& \quad=760 \times \frac{\frac{2}{0.52}}{1000} \times 18=52.615 \\
& \mathrm{P}_5=760-52.615=707.385 \mathrm{~mm} \text { of } \mathrm{Hg}
\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q35. JEE Main 2024 (01 Feb Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

<p>We have three aqueous solutions of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>NaCl</mi></math> labelled as <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>‘</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>’</mo><mo>,</mo><mo> </mo><mo>‘</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>’</mo></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>‘</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>’</mo></math> with concentration <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>,</mo><mo> </mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>01</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>001</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>,</mo></math> respectively. The value of van <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mo>’</mo></math> Haft factor <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></mfenced></math> for these solutions will be in the order.</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></msub><mo>&lt;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></msub><mo>&lt;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></msub></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></msub><mo>&lt;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></msub><mo>&lt;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></msub></math>
- **(C)** <p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></msub></math></p>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></msub><mo>&gt;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></msub><mo>&gt;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></msub></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></msub><mo>&lt;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></msub><mo>&lt;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></msub></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>It is because the extent or degree of dissociation increases with increase in dilution of a solution. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>001</mn><mo> </mo><mi>M</mi><mo> </mo><mi>N</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>C</mi><mi>l</mi></math> solution <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>'</mo><mi>C</mi><mo>'</mo></math> is most diluted as compared to the other two <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>N</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>C</mi><mi>l</mi></math> solutions. The vant Hoff factor (i) depends on extent of dilution.This confirms answer at (i) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>i</mi><mi>A</mi></msub><mo>&lt;</mo><msub><mi>i</mi><mi>B</mi></msub><mo>&lt;</mo><msub><mi>i</mi><mi>C</mi></msub></math></p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/8d479075-3b31-47c9-bc2f-e257584d26a5.png" style="width: 500px; height: 96px;"/></p>

</details>

### Q36. JEE Main 2024 (30 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The solution from the following with highest depression in freezing point/lowest freezing point is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>180</mn></math> g of acetic acid dissolved in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mo> </mo></math>of aqueous solution.
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>180</mn></math> g of acetic acid dissolved in benzene
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>180</mn></math> g of benzoic acid dissolved in benzene
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>180</mn></math> g of glucose dissolved in water

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>180</mn></math> g of acetic acid dissolved in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mo> </mo></math>of aqueous solution.**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Depression in freezing point or elevation in melting point depends upon the number of solute particles rather than the kind of solute particles. </p><p>Depression in freezing point (<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>ΔT</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub></math>) is directly proportional to number of solute particles.</p><p>Also, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>ΔT</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo> </mo></math> is directly proportional to molality.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>ΔT</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>×</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub></math></p><p>Amongst the given options acetic acid has lowest molecular mass.</p><p>The molar mass of acetic acid is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>60</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>05</mn></math> g mol<sup>-1</sup>.</p><p>Hence if the molecular mass is less, molality will be more, so the lowest freezing point will be shown by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>180</mn><mo> </mo></math>gm of acetic acid in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn></math>L aqueous solution.</p></blockquote>

</details>

---

### Q37. JEE Main 2024 (30 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

<p>What happens to freezing point of benzene when small quantity of napthalene is added to benzene?  </p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** Increases
- **(B)** Remains unchanged
- **(C)** First decreases and then increases
- **(D)** Decreases

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) Decreases**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Depression in Freezing point can be calculated as,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>∆</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>×</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>×</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math></p><p>When a non-volatile component is added to the solvent, its freezing point decreases. Here, Napthalene is a non-volatile substance which decreases the freezing point of benzene.</p><p>On addition of naphthalene to benzene there is depression in freezing point of benzene.</p><p>Hence, the answer is option D.</p>

</details>

### Q38. JEE Main 2024 (29 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The osmotic pressure of a dilute solution is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>7</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>5</mn></msup><mo> </mo><mi>Pa</mi></math> at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>273</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>. Osmotic pressure of the same solution at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>283</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>4</mn></msup><msup><mi>Nm</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>.(Nearest integer)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **73**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Osmotic pressure can be calculated as,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"></math><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>CRT</mi></math></p><p>π is the osmotic pressure</p><p>C is the molar concentration of the solute in the solution</p><p>R is the universal gas constant</p><p>Here given,</p><p> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>273</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>283</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math> </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>CRT</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mtext>By putting the values in this equation, we get,</mtext><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mn>7</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>5</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>×</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo>×</mo><mn>273</mn><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mi>CR</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>7</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>5</mn></msup></mrow><mn>273</mn></mfrac><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>CRT</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo>=</mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>7</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>5</mn></msup></mrow><mn>273</mn></mfrac><mo>×</mo><mn>283</mn><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo>=</mo><mn>72</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>56</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>4</mn></msup><msup><mi>Nm</mi><mrow><mo>−</mo><mn>2</mn><mo> </mo><mo> </mo></mrow></msup><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace></math></p>

</details>

### Q39. JEE Main 2023 (13 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

Sea water contains <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>29</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>25</mn><mo>%</mo><mi>NaCl</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>19</mn><mo>%</mo><msub><mi>MgCl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> by weight of solution. The normal boiling point of the sea water is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mmultiscripts><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mprescripts/><none/><mo>&#8728;</mo></mmultiscripts></math> (Nearest integer) Assume <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>100</mn><mo>%</mo></math> ionization for both <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>NaCl</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>MgCl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> Given: <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>52</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kg</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></math> Molar mass of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>NaCl</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>MgCl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>58</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>95</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> respectively.

<details>

<p>Molality of solution<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>moles</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>of</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>solute</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>mass</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>of</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>solvent</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>1000</mn></math></p><p>Number of moles <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>NaCl</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>29</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>25</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>58</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn></math></p><p>Number of moles of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>MgCl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>19</mn><mn>95</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>NaCl</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>51</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>75</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>1000</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>MgCl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>51</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>75</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>1000</mn></math></p><p><math mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8710;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub></math>= Elevation in boiling point</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>=</mo></math>Van&#39;t Hoff factor</p><p>m = molality</p><p><math ### Q40. JEE Main 2023 (13 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

<p>Solution of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>12</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> of non-electrolyte (A) prepared by dissolving it in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1000</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mL</mi></math> of water exerts the same osmotic pressure as that of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>05</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></math> glucose solution at the same temperature. The empirical formula of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>.</mo></math> The molecular mass of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>________g</mi><mo>.</mo></math> (Nearest integer)</p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **240**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The pressure which applied externally in the solution side to prevent the osmosis process is known as osmotic pressure. It can be denoted by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi></math>.</p><p>Since, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mo>)</mo><mrow><mi>non</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>electrolyte</mi></mrow></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>(</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mo>)</mo><mi>glucose</mi></msub></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mi>RT</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi>RT</mi></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfrac></math></p><p>Empirical mass of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>12</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>05</mn></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>12</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>05</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>240</mn></math></p>

</details>

### Q41. JEE Main 2023 (11 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>004</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>SO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></math> solution is isotonic with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>01</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></math> glucose solution. Percentage dissociation of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>SO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></math> is _____ (Nearest integer)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **75**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Osmotic pressure <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>iCRT</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>=</mo></math>Van&#39;t Hoff Factor</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>=</mo></math>Molarity</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo>=</mo></math>Same gas constant</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mo>=</mo></math>Temperature</p><p>For isotonic solutions, osmotic pressure are equal, Hence, we have</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>004</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#215;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>01</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>01</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>004</mn></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>10</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>&#945;</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>75</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>%</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>75</mn></math></p>

</details>

### Q42. JEE Main 2023 (10 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>An aqueous solution of volume <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>300</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>cm</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></math> contains <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>63</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> of protein. The osmotic pressure of the solution at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>300</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>29</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mbar</mi></math>. The molar mass of the protein is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>gmol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>.</p><p>Given : <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>083</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mi>bar</mi></msub><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **40535**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The osmotic pressure of a non-electrolytic solution can be calculated as follows,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>CRT</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mo>&#160;</mo></math>= osmotic pressure</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math> = molarity<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>Number</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>of</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>moles</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>Volume</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>of</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>solution</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>in</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>29</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>63</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi>MW</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>)</mo></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>083</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>300</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>MW</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>63</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>083</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>300</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>(</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>&#215;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>29</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>40534</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>88</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8776;</mo><mn>40535</mn></math> (Nearest integer)</p>

</details>

      <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>3</mn></math></p><p>                            <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>3</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8710;</mo><msub><mi ### Q44. JEE Main 2023 (08 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

If the boiling points of two solvents <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">Y</mi></math> (having same molecular weights) are in the ratio <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtext>2:&#160;1</mtext></math> and their enthalpy of vaporizations are in the ratio <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>:</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn></math>, then the boiling point elevation constant of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> times the boiling point elevation constant of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">Y</mi></math>. The value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> is (nearest integer).

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **8**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>The boiling point elevation constant <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub></mfenced></math> of a volatile liquid is given by the following expression
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msubsup><mi>RT</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></mrow><msub><mi>&#916;H</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi></msub></mfrac></math>

Where <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub><mo>=</mo></math>boiling point of the liquid
            <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>=</mo></math>molar mass of the liquid
            <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#916;H</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi></msub><mo>=</mo></math> Enthalpy of vaporisation</p><p>The boiling points of the two solvents are in the ratio <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>1</mn></math>, which means that the boiling point elevation constants are also in the ratio <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>1</mn></math>. Let&#39;s call the boiling point elevation constant of solvent <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi></math> as <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub><msub><mo>)</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></msub></math>, and the boiling point elevation constant of solvent <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">Y</mi><mo>&#160;</mo></math>as <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub><msub><mo>)</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi></msub></math>.
Given: <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi></msub></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mfenced><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub></mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mfenced><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub></mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi></msub></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mfenced><msub><mi>&#916;H</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi></msub></mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>&#916;H</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi></msub></mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi></msub></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfrac><msub><mfenced><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub></mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></msub><msub><mfenced><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub></mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><msup><mfenced><mfrac><mn>2</mn><mn>1</mn></mfrac></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mn>2</mn><mn>1</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>8</mn><mn>1</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>8</mn></math></p><p>Therefore, the value of m is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>8</mn></math>(nearest integer).</p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q45. JEE Main 2023 (06 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

<p>Consider the following pairs of solution which will be isotonic at the same temperature. The number of pairs of solutions is/ are _____</p><p>A. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>aq</mi><mo>.</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>NaCl</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>aq</mi><mo>.</mo></math> urea</p><p>B. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>aq</mi><mo>.</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>CaCl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>aq</mi><mo>.</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>KCl</mi></math></p><p>C. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>aq</mi><mo>.</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>AlCl</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>aq</mi><mo>.</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>Na</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>SO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></math></p><p>D. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>aq</mi><mo>.</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>KCl</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>aq</mi><mo>.</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>Al</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>(</mo><msub><mi>SO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>3</mn></msub></math></p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **4**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>A solution having the same solute and solvent concentration as a cell across the semipermeable membrane is referred to as isotonic solution.</p><p>The effective concentration of each solution can be calculated as follows,</p><p>Effective concentration <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>(</mo><msup><mi>Von</mi><mo>&#39;</mo></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Hoff</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>factor</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#215;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>NaCl</mi></math> has effective concentration <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></math> and hence it is isotonic with two molar urea solution.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>CaCl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> has effective concentration <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>3</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>and</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>KCl</mi></math> has also <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></math>, hence, both are isotonic.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>AlCl</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>&#8773;</mo><mn>6</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>and</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>Na</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>SO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mo>&#8773;</mo><mn>6</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></math>, Hence both are isotonic.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>KCl</mi><mo>&#8773;</mo><mn>5</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>and</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>Al</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>SO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>&#8773;</mo><mn>5</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></math>, Hence both are isotonic.</p><p> </p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q46. JEE Main 2023 (01 Feb Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>20</mn><mo>%</mo></math> of acetic acid is dissociated when its <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> is added to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>500</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mL</mi></math> of water. The depression in freezing point of such water is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mmultiscripts><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mprescripts/><none/><mo>&#8728;</mo></mmultiscripts></math>. Atomic mass of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></math> are <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>12</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>1</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>16</mn></math> a.m.u. respectively.</p><p>[Given : Molal depression constant and density of water are <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>86</mn><mo>&#160;&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kg</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>cm</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></math> respectively.</p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **372**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Van&#39;t Hoff factor (i) for dissociation of acetic acid can be written as follows,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>)</mo><mi>&#945;</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>=</mo></math>Number of ions</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#945;</mi><mo>=</mo></math>Degree of dissociation</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>(</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#916;T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>iK</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mspace linebreak="newline"/><msub><mi>&#916;T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>Depression</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>in</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>freezing</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>point</mi><mspace linebreak="newline"/><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>Molar</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>depression</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>constant</mi><mspace linebreak="newline"/><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>molality</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#916;T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>86</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>1000</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>60</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>500</mn></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#916;T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>372</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#916;</mi><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>372</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></math></p>

</details>
