### Q48. JEE Main 2023 (31 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

<p>Evaluate the following statements for their correctness.</p><p>A. The elevation in boiling point temperature of water will be same for <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>NaCl</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></math> urea.
B. Azeotropic mixture boil without change in their composition.
C. Osmosis always takes place from hypertonic to hypotonic solution.
D. The density of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>32</mn><mo>%</mo></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>SO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></math> solution having molarity <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>09</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></math> is approximately <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>26</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>mL</mi><mrow><mo>&#8211;</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>.
E. A negatively charged sol is obtained when <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>KI</mi></math> solution is added to silver nitrate solution.</p><p>Choose the correct answer from the options given below:</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** B and D only
- **(B)** B, D and E only
- **(C)** A and C only
- **(D)** A, B and C only

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) B and D only**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>(A) Elevation in boiling point (<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8710;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub></math>) temperature of the water is not same, as &lsquo;<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></math>&rsquo; value is different for two solutions.
(B) Azeotropic mixture: Liquid mixtures that distill over without changes in composition are called constant boiling mixtures or Azeotropes or Azeotropic mixtures.
(C) Osmosis always takes place from hypotonic (low concentration of solute) solution to hypertonic (high concentration of solute) solution.
(D) Let the mass of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>SO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></math> (<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>32</mn><mo>%</mo></math>) is 100.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo></math> Weight of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>SO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></math> = <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>32</mn></math></p><p>Moles of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>SO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfrac><mn>32</mn><mn>98</mn></mfrac></math></p><p>Now, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>09</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfrac><mn>32</mn><mrow><mn>98</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#215;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>79</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mL</mi></math></p><p>Density = <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>100</mn><mn>79</mn></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>265</mn></math>
(E) A negative charged sol is obtained when <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>KI</mi></math> solution is added to silver nitrate solution whereas positive charged sol is obtained when <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>AgNO</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math> solution is added to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>KI</mi></math> solution.</p><p>So, the correct answer is B and D only.</p>

</details>

### Q49. JEE Main 2023 (31 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

### Q50. JEE Main 2023 (30 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>Lead storage battery contains <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>38</mn><mo>%</mo></math> by weight solution of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>SO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></math>. The van&#39;t Hoff factor is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>67</mn></math> at this concentration. The temperature in Kelvin at which the solution in the battery will freeze is _____ (Nearest integer).</p><p>Given <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kg</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **243**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The depression in freezing point of the solution can be written as follows,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#916;T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>&#183;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>&#183;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math></p><p>molality (m) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">w</mi><mo>/</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">w</mi><mo>%</mo></mrow><mrow><mi>Molar</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mass</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>of</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>solute</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mn>1000</mn><mrow><mi>mass</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>of</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>solvent</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>in</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>grams</mi></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi>&#916;T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>67</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mn>38</mn><mn>98</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mn>1000</mn><mn>62</mn></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi>&#916;T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>30</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>05</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo></math> Freezing point of solution at which battery freeze<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>243</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q51. JEE Main 2023 (30 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>A solution containing <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> of a non-volatile solute in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>20</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> of water boils at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>373</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>52</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>. The molecular mass of the solute is _____ <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>gmol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>. (Nearest integer)</p><p>Given, water boils at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>373</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>,</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub></math> for water <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>52</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kg</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **100**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Elevation in boiling point:
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi>&#916;T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>373</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>52</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>373</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>52</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#916;T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub><mo>&#183;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>52</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>52</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mn>2</mn><mtext>&#160;Molar Mass&#160;of&#160;Solute&#160;</mtext></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mn>20</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p>Molar Mass of solute<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>100</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>/</mo><mi>mol</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q52. JEE Main 2023 (29 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

<p>Match List I with List II.</p><table border="1" cellpadding="1" cellspacing="1" style="width: 400px;">	<tbody>		<tr>			<td colspan="2" rowspan="1" style="text-align: center;">List I</td>			<td colspan="2" rowspan="1" style="text-align: center;">List II</td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>A.</td>			<td>van&#39;t Hoff
			factor, i</td>			<td>I.</td>			<td>Cryoscopic constant</td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>B.</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub></math></td>			<td>II.</td>			<td>Isotonic solutions</td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>C.</td>			<td>Solutions with same osmotic pressure</td>			<td>III.</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mtext>Normal&#160;molar&#160;mass</mtext><mtext>Abnormal&#160;molar&#160;mas</mtext></mfrac></math></td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>D.</td>			<td>Azeotropes</td>			<td>IV.</td>			<td>Solutions with same composition of vapour above it</td>		</tr>	</tbody></table><p>Choose the correct answer from the options given below:</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** A-III, B-I, C-II, D-IV
- **(B)** A-III, B-II, C-I, D-IV
- **(C)** A-III, B-I, C-IV, D-II
- **(D)** A-I, B-III, C-II, D-IV

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) A-III, B-I, C-II, D-IV**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>(A) van&#39;t Hoff factor, it is used in measuring colligative properties</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mtext>&#160;Normal molar mass&#160;</mtext><mtext>&#160;Abnormal molar mass&#160;</mtext></mfrac></math></p><p>(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>=</mo></math> Cryoscopic constant or molal freezing constant</p><p>(C) Solutions with same osmotic pressure are known as isotonic solutions.</p><p>(D) Solutions with same composition of vapour over them are called Azeotrope or azeotrophic mixtures.</p>

</details>

### Q53. JEE Main 2023 (29 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>Solid Lead nitrate is dissolved in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn></math> litre of water. The solution was found to boil at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>100</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>15</mn><mo>&#176;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math>. When <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>mol</mi></math> of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>NaCl</mi></math> is added to the resulting solution, it was observed that the solution froze at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>&#176;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math>. The solutbility product of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>PbCl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> formed is _____ <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></msup></math> at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>298</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>. (Nearest integer)</p><p>Given : <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kg</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>8</mn><mi>kg</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>. Assume molality to be equal to molarity in all cases.</p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **13**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Let a mole <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Pb</mi><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>NO</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msub></math> be added, dissociation of lead nitrate will result in:</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtable><mtr><mtd><mi>Pb</mi><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>NO</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msub></mtd><mtd><mo>&#8594;</mo></mtd><mtd><msup><mi>Pb</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mtd><mtd><mo>+</mo></mtd><mtd><mn>2</mn><msubsup><mi>NO</mi><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></msubsup></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi></mtd><mtd><mo>&#160;</mo></mtd><mtd><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi></mtd><mtd><mo>&#160;</mo></mtd><mtd><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi></mtd></mtr></mtable></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtable><mtr><mtd><mo>&#8710;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>15</mn><mo>=</mo></mtd><mtd><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi></mrow></mfenced></mtd><mtd><mo>&#8658;</mo></mtd><mtd><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1</mn></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mo>&#160;</mo></mtd><mtd><msubsup><mi>Pb</mi><mfenced><mi>aq</mi></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msubsup></mtd><mtd><mo>+</mo></mtd><mtd><mn>2</mn><msubsup><mi>Cl</mi><mfenced><mi>aq</mi></mfenced><mo>-</mo></msubsup><mo>&#8594;</mo><msub><mi>PbCl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mfenced></mtd></mtr></mtable></math>
During reformation of lead nitrate as a precipitate,
                                <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtable><mtr><mtd><msubsup><mi>Pb</mi><mfenced><mi>aq</mi></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msubsup></mtd><mtd><mo>+</mo></mtd><mtd><mn>2</mn><msubsup><mi>Cl</mi><mfenced><mi>aq</mi></mfenced><mo>-</mo></msubsup><mo>&#8594;</mo><msub><mi>PbCl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mfenced></mtd></mtr></mtable></math></p><p>              <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtable><mtr><mtd><mi xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#916;T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi ### Q54. JEE Main 2023 (25 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

<p>The number of pairs of the solution having the same value of the osmotic pressure from the following is
(Assume <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>100</mn><mo>%</mo></math> ionization)</p><p>A. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>500</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>5</mn></msub><mi>OH</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>aq</mi><mo>)</mo></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>25</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>KBr</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>aq</mi><mo>)</mo></math>
B. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>100</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mrow></mrow></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>CN</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mo>(</mo><mi>aq</mi><mo>)</mo></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>100</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>FeSO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>SO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mo>(</mo><mi>aq</mi><mo>)</mo></math></p><p>C. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>05</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>CN</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></math> (aq) and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>25</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>NaCl</mi></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi>aq</mi><mo>)</mo></math>
D. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>15</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>NaCl</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>aq</mi><mo>)</mo></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>BaCl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>(</mo><mi>aq</mi><mo>)</mo></math>
E. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>02</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>KCl</mi><mo>.</mo><msub><mi>MgCl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>aq</mi><mo>)</mo></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>05</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>KCl</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>aq</mi><mo>)</mo></math></p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **4**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>We know,
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>iCRT</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mo>&#8733;</mo><mi>iC</mi></math></p><p>A. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>500</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>5</mn></msub><mi>OH</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>aq</mi><mo>)</mo></math><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mi>iC</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>25</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>KBr</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>aq</mi><mo>)</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi>iC</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>25</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>
B. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>100</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mrow></mrow></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>CN</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mo>(</mo><mi>aq</mi><mo>)</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi>iC</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>100</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>FeSO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>SO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mo>(</mo><mi>aq</mi><mo>)</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi>iC</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>C. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>05</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>CN</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mfenced><mrow><mi>iC</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>05</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>25</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> (aq) and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>25</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>NaCl</mi><mfenced><mrow><mi>iC</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>25</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi>aq</mi><mo>)</mo></math></p><p>
D. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>15</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>NaCl</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>aq</mi><mo>)</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi>iC</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>15</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>BaCl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>(</mo><mi>aq</mi><mo>)</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi>iC</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>
E. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>02</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>KCl</mi><mo>.</mo><msub><mi>MgCl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>aq</mi><mo>)</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi>iC</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>02</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>05</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>KCl</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>aq</mi><mo>)</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi>iC</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>05</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></math></p>

### Q55. JEE Main 2023 (25 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>The osmotic pressure of solutions of PVC in cyclohexanone at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>300</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math> are plotted on the graph. The molar mass of PVC is  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> (Nearest integer)</p><p><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/368b80fb-646b-4164-b1bc-e93a96e30134-9894493.PNG" style="width: 218px; height: 150px;" /></p><p>(Given : <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>083</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>atm</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> )</p><p> </p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **41500**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Using osmotic pressure in ideal gas equation:
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#960;</mi><mo>=</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>&#39;</mo></msup><mi>RT</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi><mo>/</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mfrac></mfenced><mi>RT</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>&#960;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mfrac></mfenced><mfenced><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></mfrac></mfenced><mi>RT</mi><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mfenced><mfrac><mi>RT</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></mfrac></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><mi>&#960;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>RT</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></mfrac></math></p><p>If we assume given graph between <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi>&#960;</mi><mi>C</mi></mfrac></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math></p><p><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/a651bca9-9b9c-45ec-b9e5-53c7d1e17f37-9894493.PNG" style="width: 218px; height: 150px;" /></p><p>Slope <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>RT</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>083</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>300</mn></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>083</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>300</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>830</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>300</mn></mrow><mn>6</mn></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>41</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>500</mn><mi>gm</mi><mo>/</mo><mi>mole</mi></math></p><p> </p>

</details>

### Q56. JEE Main 2023 (24 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

In the depression of freezing point experiment
A. Vapour pressure of the solution is less than that of pure solvent
B. Vapour pressure of the solution is more than that of pure solvent
C. Only solute molecules solidify at the freezing point
D. Only solvent molecules solidify at the freezing point

**Options:**

- **(A)** A and D only
- **(B)** B and C only
- **(C)** A and C only
- **(D)** A only

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) A and D only**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Since the solute particles do not evaporate, the vapour pressure of the solution is lower than that of the pure solvent. The lowering of the vapour pressure depends on the number of solute particles that have been dissolved.</p><p>In the depression in freezing point experiment, it is found that the vapour pressure of a solution is less than that of pure solvent. Only solvent molecules solidify at freezing point. </p><p><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/07a4e353-cf36-474a-b49d-12df1b8cb899-9893949.PNG" style="width: 279px; height: 191px;" /></p>

</details>

### Q57. JEE Main 2022 (29 Jul Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>80</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> of solute A was dissolved in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>62</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>cm</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></math> of ethanol and freezing point of the solution was found to be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>155</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>. The molar mass of solute <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>gmol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>. [Given: Freezing point of ethanol is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>156</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>. Density of ethanol is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>80</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>cm</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></math>. Freezing point depression constant of ethanol is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>00</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kg</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>]

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **80**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Given</p><p>Mass of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>5</mn></msub><mi>OH</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>62</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>50</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> </p><p>Mass of solute = 1.80 g</p><p>Depression in freezing point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8710;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>156</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>155</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>9</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math></p><p>We know that the depression in freezing point</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#916;T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>&#215;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>9</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>1000</mn></mrow><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">w</mi></msub><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>50</mn></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">w</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>1000</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>9</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>50</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>80</mn></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q58. JEE Main 2022 (28 Jul Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>150</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> of acetic acid was contaminated with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>10</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> ascorbic acid <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>6</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>8</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></math> to lower down its freezing point by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#176;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math>. The value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></math> is____ (Nearest integer). [Given <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>9</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kg</mi><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mol</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>; Molar mass of ascorbic acid <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>176</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mol</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>]

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **15**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Given <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>150</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>of</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>solvent</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mi>COOH</mi></math></p><p>And as solute <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>10</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> ascorbic acid <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>10</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mn>176</mn></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>058</mn></math> moles</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#916;T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#176;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#916;T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>&#183;</mo></math> molality</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>9</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>058</mn></mrow><mn>150</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>1000</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#176;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>15</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#176;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math></p><p>So, value of x=15.</p></blockquote>

</details>

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The ratio of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub></math> i.e., <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub><mo>/</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>:</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi></math>. <details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **1**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>We know that</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#916;T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>iK</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mspace linebreak="newline"/><msub><mi>&#916;T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>iK</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math></p><p>And for non-volatile solute <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math>.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi>&#916;T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub><msub><mi>&#916;T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>6</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>1</mn></mfrac><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q62. JEE Main 2022 (25 Jul Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

Two solutions <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math> are prepared by dissolving <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> of non-volatile solutes <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">Y</mi></math>. respectively in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kg</mi></math> of water. The ratio of depression in freezing points for <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math> is found to be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>4</mn></math>. The ratio of molar masses of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">Y</mi></math> is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>4</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>25</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>20</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>5</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>25</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>We know that for an non-electrolyte</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8710;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>where</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>molality</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi>&#916;T</mi><mi>fx</mi></msub><msub><mi>&#916;T</mi><mi>fy</mi></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>&#183;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></msub></mrow><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>&#183;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></msub></mfrac><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi></msub></mfrac></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></msub></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></msub><mo>:</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>25</mn></math></p></blockquote>

- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>9</mn></math>

### Q64. JEE Main 2022 (29 Jun Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

Elevation in boiling point for <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn></math> molal solution of glucose in water is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>. The depression in freezing point for <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn></math> molal solution of glucose in water is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>. The ratio of molal elevation constant to molal depression constant <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub><mo>/</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub></mrow></mfenced></math> is

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **3**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Elevation in boiling point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8710;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>ik</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math></p><p>Depression in freezing point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8710;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>ik</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>ik</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>ik</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mfenced><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>Dividing both:</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>1</mn></mfrac></math></p><p>Hence, The ratio of molal elevation constant to molal depression constant is  3:1</p>

</details>

### Q65. JEE Main 2022 (29 Jun Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mL</mi></math> of acetic acid is dissolved in water to make <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></math> of solution. The depression in freezing point observed for this strength of acid is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>0198</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#176;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math>. The percentage of dissociation of the acid is (Nearest integer) [Given : Density of acetic acid is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>02</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mL</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> Molar mass of acetic acid is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>60</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>85</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kg</mi><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mol</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>]

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **5**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>02</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>224</mn><mi>gm</mi></math></p><p>Moles of acetic acid <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>0204</mn></math> moles in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></math></p><p>So molality <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>0102</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mol</mi><mo>/</mo><mi>kg</mi></math></p><p>Now <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#916;T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>&#215;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#945;</mi></math> for acetic acid</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>0198</mn><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#945;</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>85</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>0102</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#945;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>04928</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8773;</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>%</mo></math></p>

</details>

### Q67. JEE Main 2022 (27 Jun Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> of a non-volatile non-electrolyte solute is disolved in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>200</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> of two different solvents <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math> whose ebullioscopic constants are in the ratio of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>8</mn></math>. The elevation in boiling points of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math> are in the ratio <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi></mfrac><mfenced><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>:</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>. The value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi></math> is____(Nearest Integer)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **8**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Given : <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mfenced><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub></mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></msub><msub><mfenced><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub></mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>8</mn></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfrac><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>&#916;T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></msub></mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></msub><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>&#916;T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></msub></mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mfenced><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub></mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></msub><mo>&#183;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mrow><mrow><msub><mfenced><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub></mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></msub><mo>&#183;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>8</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8757;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>8</mn></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>8</mn></math> (nearest integer)</p>

</details>

### Q68. JEE Main 2022 (26 Jun Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The osmotic pressure exerted by a solution prepared by dissolving <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> of protein of molar mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>60</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kg</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>200</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mL</mi></math> of water at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>27</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#176;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math> is____<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Pa</mi></math>. [integer value] (use <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>083</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></math> bar <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **415**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Osmotic pressure is the minimum pressure which needs to be applied to a solution to prevent the inward flow of its pure solvent across a semipermeable membrane.It is a colligative property.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>CRT</mi><mspace linebreak="newline"/><mi>Where</mi><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>Osmotic</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>pressure</mi><mspace linebreak="newline"/><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>Molarity</mi><mspace linebreak="newline"/><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>Temperature</mi><mo>.</mo></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>CRT</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced close="]" open="["><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>3</mn></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>60</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>3</mn></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>200</mn></mrow></mfrac></mfenced><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>31</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>300  &#215; 1000</mn><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>415</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi></msub></math></p>

</details>

### Q69. JEE Main 2022 (26 Jun Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

A <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn></math> percent solution of potassium chloride was found to freeze at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>24</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#176;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math>. The percentage dissociation of potassium chloride is (Nearest integer)
(Molal depression constant for water is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>80</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kg</mi><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mol</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> and molar mass of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>KCl</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>74</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mol</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **98**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>KCl</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>Cl</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>-</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>-</mo></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#945;</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#945;</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#945;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#945;</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8710;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>&#183;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>24</mn><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>&#945;</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>80</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>74</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mn>1000</mn><mrow><mn>99</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>98</mn><mspace linebreak="newline"/><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>%</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>98</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>%</mo></math></p>

</details>

### Q70. JEE Main 2022 (25 Jun Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>Solute A associates in water. When <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>7</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> of solute <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> is dissolved in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>42</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> of water, it depresses the freezing point by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#176;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math>. The percentage association of solute <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> in water, is</p><p>[Given: Molar mass of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>93</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mol</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>. Molal depression constant of water is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>86</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kg</mi><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mol</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>]</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>50</mn><mo>%</mo></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>60</mn><mo>%</mo></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>70</mn><mo>%</mo></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>80</mn><mo>%</mo></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>80</mn><mo>%</mo></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Depression in freezing point,<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#916;T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>iK</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>&#215;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>=</mo><mi>ix</mi><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>86</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>7</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>1000</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>93</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>42</mn></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>33</mn></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">&#946;</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">&#946;</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#946;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>8</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>%</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#946;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>80</mn></math></p><p>Hence, the percentage association of solute A in water is 80.</p>

</details>

### Q71. JEE Main 2022 (24 Jun Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The osmotic pressure of blood is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>7</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>47</mn></math> bar at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>300</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>. To inject glucose to a patient intravenously, it has to be isotonic with blood. The concentration of glucose solution in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>gL</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> is (Molar mass of glucose <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>180</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mol</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>083</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>Lbar</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mol</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>) (Nearest integer)

<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **54**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Osmotic pressure, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>iCRT</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>=</mo></math> van&#39;t hoff factor</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>=</mo></math> concentration in molarity</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo>=</mo></math> universal gas constant</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mo>=</mo></math> temperature</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>=</mo></math> Molarity</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>moles</mi><mi>Litre</mi></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">&#969;</mi><mi>GM&#969;</mi></mfrac><mo>/</mo><mi>Litre</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>iCRT</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>for</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>glucose</mi></math> as it neither associates nor dissociates in solution.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>7</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>47</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>083</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>300</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>7</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>47</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mi>&#969;</mi><mn>180</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>083</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>300</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#969;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>7</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>47</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>180</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>083</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>300</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>54</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>/</mo><mi>Lit</mi></math></p>

### Q72. JEE Main 2021 (31 Aug Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>22</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> of an organic acid is separately dissolved in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>100</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> of benzene <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kg</mi><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mol</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>100</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> of acetone <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>7</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kg</mi><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mol</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced><mo>.</mo></math> The acid is known to dimerize in benzene but remain as a monomer in acetone. The boiling point of the solution in acetone increases by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>17</mn><mo>&#176;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>.</mo></math> The increase in boiling point of solution in benzene in<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mmultiscripts><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mprescripts/><none/><mo>&#8728;</mo></mmultiscripts></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>.</mo></math> The value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></math> is (Nearest integer)
[Atomic mass : <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>12</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>16</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>0</mn></math>]

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **13**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#916;T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub><mo>&#215;</mo></math>molality ; <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">w</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi>GMM</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mn>1000</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">w</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfrac></math>
For Acetone solution
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>17</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>7</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>22</mn></mrow><mi>GMM</mi></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mn>1000</mn><mn>100</mn></mfrac></math>
GMM of substance <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>122</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>gm</mi><mo>/</mo><mi>mol</mi></math>
For Benzene solution
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#916;T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub><mo>&#215;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>22</mn></mrow><mn>122</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mn>1000</mn><mn>100</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>13</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>13</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>13</mn></math>

</details>

### Q73. JEE Main 2021 (31 Aug Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Which one of the following <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></math> aqueous solutions will exhibit the largest freezing point depression?

**Options:**

- **(A)** glycine
- **(B)** hydrazine
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>KHSO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></math>
- **(D)** glucose

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>KHSO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#916;T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>ik</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>
As <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> is same for all electrolyte so
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#916;T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#945;</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>
Greater the value of i greater is depression in freezing point
For <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>KHSO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn></math>
And for Glucose, Ethylele Glycol &amp; glycerol <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math>

</details>

### Q74. JEE Main 2021 (27 Aug Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>40</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> of glucose (Molar mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>180</mn><mo>)</mo></math> is mixed with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>200</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mL</mi></math> of water. The freezing point of solution is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>_</mi><mi>_K</mi></math>. (Nearest integer)
[Given : <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>86</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kg</mi><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mol</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>;</mo></math> Density of water <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>00</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>cm</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo>;</mo></math> Freezing point of water <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="]" open=""><mrow><mo>=</mo><mn>273</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>15</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **271**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Molality <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mfenced><mfrac><mn>40</mn><mn>180</mn></mfrac></mfenced><mi>mol</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>Kg</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mfrac><mn>10</mn><mn>9</mn></mfrac></mfenced></math> molal</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>&#916;</mi><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>-</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi><mo>'</mo></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>86</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mn>10</mn><mn>9</mn></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi><mo>'</mo></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mn>273</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>15</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>86</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mn>10</mn><mn>9</mn></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>271</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>08</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8776;</mo><mn>271</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math> (nearest-integer)</p>

</details>

### Q75. JEE Main 2021 (27 Aug Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>200</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mL</mi></math> of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>HCl</mi></math> is mixed with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>300</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mL</mi></math> of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>NaOH</mi><mo>.</mo></math> The molar heat of neutralization of this reaction is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>57</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kJ</mi><mo>.</mo></math> The increase in temperature in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mmultiscripts><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mprescripts/><none/><mo>&#8728;</mo></mmultiscripts></math> of the system on mixing is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>.</mo></math> The value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></math> is (Nearest integer)</p><p>[Given: Specific heat of water <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>18</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> </p><p>Density of water<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>00</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>cm</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></math>]</p><p>(Assume no volume change on mixing)</p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **82**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>milimole <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><munder><mi>HCl</mi><munder><mn>40</mn><mn>0</mn></munder></munder><mo>+</mo><munder><mi>NaOH</mi><munder><mn>30</mn><mn>0</mn></munder></munder><mo>&#10230;</mo><munder><mi>NaCl</mi><munder><mo>-</mo><mn>30</mn></munder></munder><mo>+</mo><munder><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow><munder><mo>-</mo><mo>-</mo></munder></munder></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#916;H</mi><mtext>neut&#160;</mtext></msub><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>57</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1</mn><mi>KJ</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#916;H</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>57</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>30</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>3</mn></msup></mrow></mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1713</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">q</mi><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#183;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mo>.</mo><mi>&#916;T</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1713</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>500</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>18</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mi>&#916;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#916;T</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>8196</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>81</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>96</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>&#8776;</mo><mn>82</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math></p>

</details>
