### Q76. JEE Main 2021 (26 Aug Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>83</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> of ethylene glycol dissolved in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>625</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> of water. The freezing point of the solution is ..... K. (Nearest integer)
[Use : Molal Freezing point depression constant of water <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>86</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kg</mi><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mol</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>
Freezing point of water <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>273</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>
Atomic masses : <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>:</mo><mn>12</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>0</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">u</mi><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>:</mo><mn>16</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>0</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">u</mi><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>:</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>0</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">u</mi><mo>]</mo></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **269**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#916;</mi><msub><mi>T</mi><mi>f</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>iK</mi><mi>f</mi></msub><mo>&#215;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>86</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>83</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>1000</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>62</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>625</mn></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#916;T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>984</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math></p><p>So, freezing point of solution <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>273</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>984</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>269</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>016</mn><mo>&#8771;</mo><mn>269</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q77. JEE Main 2021 (26 Aug Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

Of the following four aqueous solutions, the total number of those solutions whose freezing point is lower than that of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>10</mn><msub><mi>MC</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>5</mn></msub><mi>OH</mi></math> is (Integer answer)
(i) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>10</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>Ba</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>PO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msub></math>
(ii) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>10</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>Na</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>SO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></math>
(iii) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>10</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>KCl</mi></math>
(iv) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>10</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>Li</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><msub><mi>PO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **4**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#916;T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>ik</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math></p><p>As <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> is the same for all solutions so <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#916;T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub></math> depends on Van&#39;t Hoff factor (i). Greater is i small is freezing point.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtable columnalign="left" columnlines="solid" frame="solid" rowlines="solid"><mtr><mtd><mtext>&#160;Compound&#160;</mtext></mtd><mtd><mtext>&#160;i&#160;</mtext></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>5</mn></msub><mi>OH</mi></mtd><mtd><mn>1</mn></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><msub><mi>Ba</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>PO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msub></mtd><mtd><mn>5</mn></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><msub><mi>Na</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>SO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mtd><mtd><mn>3</mn></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mi>KCl</mi></mtd><mtd><mn>2</mn></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><msub><mi>Li</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><msub><mi>PO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mtd><mtd><mn>4</mn></mtd></mtr></mtable></math></p><p>So the given solutions have a lower freezing point than ethanol.</p>

</details>

### Q78. JEE Main 2021 (27 Jul Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

In a solvent <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>50</mn><mo>%</mo></math> of an acid HA dimerizes and the rest dissociates. The van&#39;t Hoff factor of the acid is _____<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math> (Round off to the nearest integer)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **125**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Let initial moles of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>HA</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>HA</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mrow><mn>2</mn></msub><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>25</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>HA</mi><mo>&#8652;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>+</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi></math></p><p>Now, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mtext>&#160;final&#160;moles&#160;</mtext><mtext>&#160;initial&#160;moles&#160;</mtext></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>25</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>25</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>125</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math></p>

</details>

### Q79. JEE Main 2021 (27 Jul Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>46</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> of a biopolymer dissolved in a <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>100</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mL</mi></math> water at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>300</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math> exerted an osmotic pressure of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>42</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mi>bar</mi></math> </p><p>The molar mass of the biopolymer is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>4</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>.</mo></math> (Round off to the Nearest Integer)
[Use: <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>083</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></math> bar <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced open="" close="]"><mrow><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></math></p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **15**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>CRT</mi><mo>;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mo>=</mo></math> osmotic pressure
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>=</mo></math> molarity
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mo>=</mo></math> Temperature of solution let the molar mass be</p><p>Molar mass of polymer = <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>gm</mi><mo>/</mo><mi>mol</mi></math> </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>42</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>bar</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>46</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>gm</mi><mo>/</mo><mi>mol</mi></mrow></mfrac></mfenced><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#8467;</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>083</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#8467;</mi><mo>.</mo><mi>bar</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>mol</mi><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></mrow></mfrac></mfenced><mo>&#215;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>300</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>15</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>02</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>4</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>/</mo><mi>mol</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q80. JEE Main 2021 (25 Jul Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

When <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>00</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> of a substance <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi></math>&#39; is dissolved in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>100</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>CCl</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></math>, it raises the boiling point by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>60</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>. The molar mass of the substance &#39; <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi><mo>'</mo></msup></math> is  ___ g <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>.
(Nearest integer).
[Given <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub></math> for <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>CCl</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced open="" close="]"><mrow><mn>5</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kg</mi><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mol</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **250**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#916;T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub><mo>&#215;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>molality</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>60</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>/</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>100</mn><mo>/</mo><mn>100</mn></mrow></mfrac></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>Molar</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mass</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>250</mn></math>.</p>

</details>

### Q81. JEE Main 2021 (22 Jul Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Which one of the following <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>06</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></math> aqueous solutions has lowest freezing point ?

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Al</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>SO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>3</mn></msub></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>6</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>12</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>6</mn></msub></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>KI</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>SO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Al</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>SO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>3</mn></msub></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi><mo>&#8728;</mo></msubsup><mo>-</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>iK</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>&#183;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub></math> = Freezing point of the solution.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>=</mo></math>Van &#39;t Hoff factor</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub></math> = molal freezing point depression constant</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>=</mo></math>molality
For minimum <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub></math>,  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></math> should have the maximum value.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtable><mtr><mtd><msub><mi>Al</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>SO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>3</mn></msub></mtd><mtd><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>6</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>12</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>6</mn></msub></mtd><mtd><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mi>KI</mi></mtd><mtd><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>SO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mtd><mtd><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn></mtd></mtr></mtable></math></p>

</details>

### Q82. JEE Main 2021 (18 Mar Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

<p>A solute <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> dimerizes in water. The boiling point of a <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn></math> molal solution of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>100</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>52</mn><mmultiscripts><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mprescripts></mprescripts><none></none><mo>&#8728;</mo></mmultiscripts></math>. The percentage association of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> is ___ .
(Round off to the Nearest integer)</p><p> Use : <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub></math> for water <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>52</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kg</mi><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mol</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> </p><p>            Boiling point of water <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>100</mn><mo>&#176;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math></p>

<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#916;</mi><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub><mo>-</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi><mn>0</mn></msubsup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>100</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>52</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>100</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>52</mn><mo>&#176;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mi>&#945;</mi><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8757;</mo><mi>&#916;</mi><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>iK</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub><mo>&#215;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>52</mn><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mi>&#945;</mi><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>52</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>2</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math></p><p>So, percentage association <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>100</mn><mo>%</mo></math>.</p>

</details>

### Q86. JEE Main 2021 (26 Feb Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

When <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>12</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> of benzoic acid is dissolved in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>100</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> of water, the freezing point of solution was found to be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>93</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#176;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>86</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kg</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></math>. The number <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi></mfenced></math> of benzoic acid molecules associated (assuming <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>100</mn><mo>%</mo></math> association) is _.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **2**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#916;T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>&#215;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>93</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>86</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>12</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>122</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>100</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>1000</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>93</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>86</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mfenced><mrow><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">&#945;</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mfenced><mrow><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>1</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></math></p>

</details>

### Q87. JEE Main 2021 (25 Feb Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

If a compound <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>AB</mi></math> dissociates to the extent of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>75</mn><mo>%</mo></math> in an aqueous solution, the molality of the solution which shows a <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math> rise in the boiling point of the solution is ___ molal. (Rounded-off to the nearest integer) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><msub><mi>K</mi><mi>b</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>52</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kg</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></math>.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **3**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>75</mn><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>75</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>75</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>75</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#916;</mi><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>ik</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math></p><p>or <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>75</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>52</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math></p><p>or, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>75</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>52</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>74</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo></math> nearest integer answer will be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn></math></p>

</details>

### Q88. JEE Main 2021 (25 Feb Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn></math> molal aqueous solution of an electrolyte <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>60</mn><mo>%</mo></math> ionised. The boiling point of the solution at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>atm</mi></math> is _____ <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>. (Rounded-off to the nearest integer) [Given <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub></math> for <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>52</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kg</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>]

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **375**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#916;T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>iK</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>&#945;</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>52</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>1</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>52</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>768</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>768</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>373</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>15</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>374</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>918</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>375</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math></p><p>Hence answer is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mn>375</mn></mfenced></math></p>

</details>

### Q89. JEE Main 2021 (24 Feb Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>6</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>6</mn></msub></math> freezes at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#176;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>.</mo></math> The temperature at which a solution of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>10</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>10</mn></msub></math> in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>200</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>6</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>6</mn></msub></math> freeze is _______<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mmultiscripts><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mprescripts></mprescripts><none></none><mo>&#8728;</mo></mmultiscripts><mo>.</mo></math> (nearest integer value), (The molal freezing point depression constant of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>6</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>6</mn></msub></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>12</mn><mo>&#176;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>/</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>.</mo></math>)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **1**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Pure Solvent: <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>6</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>6</mn></msub><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">&#8467;</mi></mfenced></math></p><p>Given: <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>&#176;</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#176;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>12</mn><mo>&#176;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>/</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math></p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/1093e1eb-3a57-46fb-8153-034c46670659-image.png" style="width: 155px; height: 105px;" /></p><p>:Solute is non dissociative</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8757;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#916;</mi><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>&#215;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfenced><mrow><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi><mn>0</mn></msubsup><mo>-</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi><mo>&#39;</mo></msubsup></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>12</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mfenced><mfrac><mn>10</mn><mn>58</mn></mfrac></mfenced><mrow><mfenced><mfrac><mn>200</mn><mn>1000</mn></mfrac></mfenced><mi>kg</mi></mrow></mfrac><mi>mol</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>-</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi><mo>&#39;</mo></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>12</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>10</mn></mrow><mn>58</mn></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi><mo>&#39;</mo></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>086</mn><mo>&#176;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>&#8771;</mo><mn>1</mn><mmultiscripts><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mprescripts></mprescripts><none></none><mo>&#8728;</mo></mmultiscripts></math></p>

</details>

### Q90. JEE Main 2021 (24 Feb Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>When <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>9</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>45</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>ClCH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi>COOH</mi></math> is added to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>500</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mL</mi></math> of water, its freezing point drops by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#176;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>.</mo></math> The dissociation constant of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>ClCH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi>COOH</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo>.</mo></math> The value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></math> is off to the nearest integer)</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>86</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kg</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></math></p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **36**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>ClCH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi>COOH</mi><mo>&#8652;</mo><msub><mi>ClCH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msup><mi>COO</mi><mo>&#8854;</mo></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>+</mo></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>)</mo><mi>&#945;</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>&#945;</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#916;T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>.</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>=</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>86</mn><mo>)</mo><mfenced><mfrac><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mn>9</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>45</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>94</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfrac></mfenced><mfenced><mfrac><mn>500</mn><mn>1000</mn></mfrac></mfenced></mfrac></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>72</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>28</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>72</mn></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>32</mn><mn>93</mn></mfrac></math></p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/2b207293-4412-460d-8d74-5f34b0a7f613-image.png" style="width: 273px; height: 60px;" /></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mstyle displaystyle="true"><msup><mfenced><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi>&#945;</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></mstyle><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>&#8722;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi>&#945;</mi></mstyle></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><msup><mi>&#945;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mstyle><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mn>1</mn><mo>&#8722;</mo><mi>&#945;</mi></mstyle></mfrac><mspace linebreak="newline"/><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mn>0</mn><mo>.1</mo></mstyle><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mn>500</mn><mo>/</mo><mn>1000</mn></mstyle></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.2</mo></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.2</mo><msup><mfenced><mrow><mn>32</mn><mo>/</mo><mn>93</mn></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>32</mn><mo>/</mo><mn>93</mn></mrow></mfenced></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.2</mo><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mfenced><mn>32</mn></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>93</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>61</mn></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.036</mo></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>36</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></math></p>

</details>

### Q91. JEE Main 2020 (06 Sep Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The elevation of boiling point of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>10</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> aqueous <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>CrCl</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>.</mo><msub><mi>xNH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math> solution is two times that of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>05</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> aqueous <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>CaCl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> solution. The value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8230;</mo><mo>&#8230;</mo><mo>&#8230;</mo><mo>&#8230;</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo></math>
[Assume <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>100</mn><mo>%</mo></math> ionisation of the complex and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>CaCl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>,</mo></math> coordination number of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Cr</mi></math> as <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>6</mn><mo>,</mo></math> and that all <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math> molecules are present inside the coordination sphere]

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **5**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>05</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> aqueous <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>CaCl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>15</mn></math>molal solution of non electrolyte</p><p>Since the elevation in boiling point is double so the molality of given solution of complex <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>3</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math></p><p>Hence the complex must be producing three ions in the solution, so its formula <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Cr</mi><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>5</mn></msub><mi>Cl</mi></mrow></mfenced><msub><mi>Cl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math></p><p>SO <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>X</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q92. JEE Main 2020 (04 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The osmotic pressure of a solution of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>NaCl</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>10</mn></math> atm and that of a glucose solution is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>20</mn></math> atm. The osmotic pressure of a solution formed by mixing <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></math> of the sodium chloride solution with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></math> of the glucose solution is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></math> atm. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></math> is ________ (nearest integer)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **167**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>iCRT</mi></math></p><p>On mixing two solution <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mi>final</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mi>final</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mfenced><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced><mn>3</mn></mfrac></math></p><p>            <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>500</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>100</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mi>atm</mi></math></p><p>So,      <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>167</mn><mo>&#160;</mo></math></p>

</details>

### Q93. JEE Main 2020 (03 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

If <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>250</mn><msup><mi>cm</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></math>of an aqueous solution containing <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>73</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> of a protein <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> is isotonic with one litre of another aqueous solution containing <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>65</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> of a protein <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>,</mo></math> at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>298</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>, the ratio of the molecular masses of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>_</mo><mo>_</mo><mo>_</mo><mo>_</mo><mo>_</mo><mo>_</mo><mo>_</mo><mo>_</mo><mo>_</mo><mo>_</mo><mo>_</mo><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math> (to the nearest integer).

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **177**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>For isotonic solution</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>  {For protein <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math>}</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>73</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>1000</mn></mrow><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></msub><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>250</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>65</mn></mrow><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></msub><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>73</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>65</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>77</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>177</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math></p>

</details>

### Q94. JEE Main 2020 (02 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The size of a raw mango shrinks to a much smaller size when kept in a concentrated salt solution. Which one of the following process can explain this?

**Options:**

- **(A)** Osmosis
- **(B)** Dialysis
- **(C)** Diffusion
- **(D)** Reverse osmosis

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) Osmosis**

**Detailed Solution:**

When mango kept in concentrate salt solution then solvent (water) flow from mango to concentrate solution that&#39;s why mango shrinks this is called. &quot;Osmosis&quot;

</details>

### Q95. JEE Main 2020 (09 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

A cylinder containing an ideal gas ( <math><mn>0.1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></math> of <math><mn>1.0</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></math> ) is in thermal equilibrium with a large volume of <math><mn>0.5</mn></math> molal aqueous solution of ethylene glycol at its freezing point. If the stoppers <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub></math> and <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></math> (as shown in the figure) are suddenly withdrawn, the volume of the gas in litres after equilibrium is achieved will be _____________.
(Given, <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></mrow></msub><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">w</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>2.0</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><mfenced open="" separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0.08</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></math>

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/685ebb1e-29c0-4048-adfc-7bfc25dbe9d3.png" style="height: 144px; width: 200px;" />

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **2.18**

**Detailed Solution:**

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/f90fef14-eba2-4d13-a104-2e1736e1bba9.png" />
<math><mi>k</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>2.0</mn></math>
<math><mi>m</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0.5</mn><mi>m</mi></math>
<math><mo>∆</mo><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>r</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>k</mi><mi>m</mi></math>
<math><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>i</mi><mi>n</mi><mi>t</mi><mi>i</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>l</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>272</mn><mi>K</mi></math>
<math><mi>n</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0.1</mn><mi>m</mi><mi>o</mi><mi>l</mi></math>
<math><mi>V</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mi>d</mi><msup><mrow><mi>m</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></math>
<math><msub><mrow><mi>P</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>g</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>s</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>n</mi><mi>R</mi><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>0.1</mn><mo>×</mo><mn>0.08</mn><mo>×</mo><mn>272</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>
After releasing piston <math><mi></mi><msub><mrow><mi>P</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><msub><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mrow><mi>P</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><msub><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></math>
<math><mn>2.176</mn><mo>×</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>×</mo><msub><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></math>
<math><msub><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>2.176</mn><mi></mi><mi>d</mi><msup><mrow><mi>m</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></math>

</details>

### Q96. JEE Main 2020 (09 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

How much amount of <math><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></math> should be added to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>600</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> of water <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#961;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1.00</mn><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow><mo>/</mo><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></mrow></mrow></mrow></mfenced></math> to decrease the freezing point of water to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>0.2</mn><msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math>? ________. (The freezing point depression constant for water <math><mo>=</mo><mn>2&#160;</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mi mathvariant="normal">K&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> )

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **1.74**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math><mo>&#8710;</mo><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>f</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0.2</mn><mo>&#176;</mo><mi>C</mi></math>
<math><mo>&#8710;</mo><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>f</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>i</mi><msub><mi>k</mi><mi>f</mi></msub><mi>m</mi></math>
<math><mn>0.2</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>&#969;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>58.5</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1000</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>600</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>
<math><mi>&#969;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>0.2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>58.5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>600</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>1000</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1.2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>58.5</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>40</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>01.76&#160;</mn><mi></mi><mi>g</mi></math></p><p>Freezing point depression is the phenomena that describes why adding a solute to a solvent results in the lowering of the freezing point of the solvent.</p>

</details>

### Q97. JEE Main 2019 (12 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

A solution is prepared by dissolving 0.6 g of urea (molar mass <math><mo>=</mo><mn>60</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>) and <math><mn>1.8</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> of glucose (molar mass <math><mo>=</mo><mn>180</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>) in 100 mL of water at <math><msup><mrow><mn>27</mn></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>.</mo></math> The osmotic pressure of the solution is:
(<math><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0.08206</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></math><math><mtext> atm</mtext></math> <math><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>)

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mn>8.2</mn></math> atm
- **(B)** <math><mn>2.46</mn></math> atm
- **(C)** <math><mn>4.92</mn></math> atm
- **(D)** <math><mn>1.64</mn></math> atm

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math><mn>4.92</mn></math> atm**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></math> factor of glucose and urea are 1 : 1 respectively.</p><p>Moles of glucose <math><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">weight</mi></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">Molecular wieght</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1.8</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>180</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p>Moles of urea <math><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>0.6</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>60</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p>Total mole of solute <math><mo>=</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p>Concentration of solution <math><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>100</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>0.2</mn></math></p><p>Osmotic pressure <math><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math></p><p><math><mo>=</mo><mn>0.2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>0.0821</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>300</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>4.926</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q98. JEE Main 2019 (10 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<math><mn>1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> of a non-volatile non-electrolyte solute is dissolved in <math><mn>100</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> of two different solvents <math><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> and B whose ebullioscopic constants are in the ratio of <math><mn>1</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>5</mn></math>. The ratio of the elevation in their boiling points, <math><mfrac><mrow><mo>&#8710;</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></mrow></msub><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mo>&#8710;</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></mrow></msub><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfrac></math>, is: (assuming they have the same molar mass)

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mn>10</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>1</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math><mn>1</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>5</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math><mn>1</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>0.2</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math><mn>5</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>1</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math><mn>1</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>5</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Elevation in Boiling point  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8710;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub><mo>&#215;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>

For non-electrolyte, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math></p><p>m= Molar mass of solute</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>molality</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>of</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>solution</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>1</mn><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>molality</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>of</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>solution</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8710;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mn>100</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>1000</mn><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>10</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mfrac></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8710;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mn>100</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>1000</mn><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>10</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mfrac></math>

### Q100. JEE Main 2019 (09 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Molal depression constant for a solvent is <math><mn>4.0</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>.</mo></math> The depression in the freezing point of the solvent for <math><mn>0.03</mn><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> solution of <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msub></math> is:
(Assume complete dissociation of the electrolyte)

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mn>0.36</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math><mn>0.24</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math><mn>0.18</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math><mn>0.12</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math><mn>0.36</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Depression in Freezing Point <math><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></mrow></msub></math></p><p><math><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></mrow></msub><mo>&#215;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math></p><p>Molality <math><mo>=</mo><mn>0.03</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></mrow><mo>/</mo><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow></mrow></math></p><p><math><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>;</mo><mtext>&#8201;&#8201;</mtext><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow><mo>/</mo><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></mrow></mrow></math></p><p><math><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>0.03</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0.36</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q101. JEE Main 2019 (09 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The osmotic pressure of a dilute solution of an ionic compound <math><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">Y</mi></math> in water is four times that of a solution <math><mn>0.01</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></math> in water. Assuming complete dissociation of the given ionic compounds in water, the concentration of <math><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">Y</mi></math> ( <math><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> ) in solution is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mn>4</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math><mn>16</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math><mn>4</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math><mn>6</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math><mn>6</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Osmotic pressure <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math></p><p><math><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>=</mo></math>Van&#39;t hoff factor</p><p>According to question,</p><p>for XY <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> <math><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mi>XY</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mi>BaCl</mi></msub><mn>2</mn></msub></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>0.01</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>0.01</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0.06</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q102. JEE Main 2019 (12 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Molecules of benzoic acid <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>6</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>5</mn></msub><mi>COOH</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> dimerise in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>30</mn><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> of benzene. &#39; <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">w</mi></math> &#39; <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> of benzoic acid shows a depression in freezing point equal to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2&#160;</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>. If the percentage association of the acid to form dimer in the solution is <math><mn>80</mn></math>, then <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">w</mi></math> is: <math><mo>(</mo></math> Given that <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>Kmol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>, molar mass of benzoic acid <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>122&#160;</mn><msup><mi>gmol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>)</mo></math>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mn>1.0</mn><mtext> </mtext><mi>g</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math><mn>2.4</mn><mtext> </mtext><mi>g</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math><mn>1.8</mn><mtext> </mtext><mi>g</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math><mn>1.5</mn><mtext> </mtext><mi>g</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math><mn>2.4</mn><mtext> </mtext><mi>g</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#916;T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>&#215;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></math> <math><mo>&#8230;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>i</mi><mo>)</mo></math>

Molecular weight of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>6</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>5</mn></msub><mi>COOH</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>112</mn></math>

n = 2  because benzoic acid is dimerised, &beta; = 80 % = 0.8  

Here <math><mi>i</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>n</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mi>&#946;</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></math>

<math><mi>i</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mi>&#160;</mi><mn>0.8</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></math>

<math><mo>=</mo><mn>0.6</mn></math>

Put the value in equation <math><mo>(</mo><mi>i</mi><mo>)</mo></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtext>2=0</mtext><mtext>.6&#215;</mtext><mfrac><mfrac><mrow><mtext>wt</mtext><mtext>.</mtext><mtext>of&#160;benzoic&#160;acid</mtext></mrow><mtext>112</mtext></mfrac><mfrac><mtext>30</mtext><mtext>1000</mtext></mfrac></mfrac><mtext>&#215;5</mtext></math>

weight  of benzoic acid <math><mo>=</mo><mn>2.4&#160;</mn><mi>g</mi></math>

</details>

### Q103. JEE Main 2019 (12 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The freezing point of a <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">%</mi></math> aqueous solution of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi></math> is equal to the freezing point of a <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>12</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">%</mi></math> aqueous solution of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">Y</mi></math>. If the molecular weight of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>, then the molecular weight of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">Y</mi></math> will be

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Freezing point depression refers to the lowering of the freezing point of solvent upon the addition of solute.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mfenced separators="|"><msub><mi>&#916;T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub></mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mfenced separators="|"><msub><mi>&#916;T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub></mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">Y</mi></msub></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi></msub></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>1000</mn></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>96</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>12</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>1000</mn></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>88</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>.27</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8771;</mo><mn>3</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q104. JEE Main 2019 (11 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

$\mathrm{K}_{2} \mathrm{HgI}_{4}$ is $40 \%$ ionised in aqueous solution. The value of its van't Hoff factor (i) is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** 1.6
- **(B)** 1.8
- **(C)** 20
- **(D)** 22

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) 1.8**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\mathrm{K}_{2} \mathrm{Hgl}_{4} \rightleftharpoons 2 \mathrm{~K}^{+}+\left[\mathrm{Hgl}_{4}\right]^{2-} ; \mathrm{n}=3$

$$

\begin{aligned}

\because & \alpha=\frac{i-1}{n-1} ; 0.4=\frac{\mathrm{i}-1}{3-1} \\

& i=1.8

\end{aligned}

$$

</details>

### Q105. JEE Main 2019 (11 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

The freezing point of a diluted milk sample is found to be $-0.2^{\circ} \mathrm{C},$ while it should have been $-0.5^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$ for pure milk. How much water has been added to pure milk to make the diluted sample?

**Options:**

- **(A)** 1 cup of water to 2 cups of pure milk
- **(B)** 3 cups of water to 2 cups of pure milk
- **(C)** 1 cup of water to 3 cups of pure milk
- **(D)** 2 cups of water to 3 cups of pure milk

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) 3 cups of water to 2 cups of pure milk**

**Detailed Solution:**

Freezing point of diluted milk $=-0.2^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$

$$

\Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{f}}^{*}=0.2^{\circ} \mathrm{C}

$$

Free ring point ol pure milk $=-0.5^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$

$$

\Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{f}}=0.5^{\circ} \mathrm{C}

$$

$\frac{\Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{f}}}{\Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{f}}^{\prime}}=\frac{\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{f}} \times m}{\mathrm{~K}_{\mathrm{f}} \times m^{\prime}} ; m=\frac{\text { mole of solute }}{\text { mass of solvent }(\mathrm{kg})}$

Moles of solute are same in both samples.

$$

\therefore \frac{0.5}{0.2}=\frac{\mathrm{W}^{n}}{\mathrm{~W}}

$$

$\frac{\mathrm{W}^{\prime}}{\mathrm{W}}=\frac{5}{2} ; \mathrm{W}^{\prime}=\frac{5}{2} \mathrm{~W}$

2 cups of pure milk is mixed with 3 cups of water to make

5 cups of diluted milk.

</details>
