### Q106. JEE Main 2019 (10 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The elevation in boiling point for <math><mn>1</mn></math> molal solution of glucose is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>. The depression in freezing point for <math><mn>2</mn></math> molal solution of glucose in the same solvent is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2&#160;</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>.</mo></math> The relation between <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub></math> is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.5</mo><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.5</mo><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Freezing point depression refers to the lowering of the freezing point of solvent upon the addition of solute. <em>Boiling point elevation</em> refers to the increase in the <em>boiling point</em> of a solvent upon the addition of a solute.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8710;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>iK</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>iK</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></mfenced></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8710;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>iK</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>iK</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mfenced><mi>ii</mi></mfenced></math>

Dividing <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi>ii</mi></mfrac></mfenced></math>,

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q107. JEE Main 2019 (09 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

A solution containing <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>62</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> ethylene glycol in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>250</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> water is cooled to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math> . If <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub></math> for water is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1.86</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi>kg</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> , the amount of water (in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> ) separated as ice is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mn>48</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math><mn>64</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math><mn>16</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math><mn>32</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math><mn>64</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Molecular weight of ethylene glycol

<math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>C</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><msub><mrow><mi>H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></msub><msub><mrow><mi>O</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>62</mn></math>

Moles of ethylene glycol <math><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>W</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>M</mi><mo>.</mo><mi>W</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>62</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>62</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math>

<math><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mo>&#8710;</mo><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>f</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>i</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><msub><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>f</mi></mrow></msub><mo>&#215;</mo><mi>m</mi></math>

<math><mo>&#8756;</mo><mo>&#8710;</mo><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>f</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>-</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>10</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn></math>

<math><mi>i</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math> for ethylene glycol

<math><mo>&#8710;</mo><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>f</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>86</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>250</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>1000</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math><mo>&#8710;</mo><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>f</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>1.86</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>7.44</mn></math>

As <math><mo>&#8710;</mo><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>f</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn></math> it implies that some amount of water has frozen and this has led to a greater depression of freezing point.

<math><mn>10</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>1.86</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>62</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>62</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi>W</mi><mi>t</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><msub><mrow><mi>H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi>O</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1000</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math><msub><mrow><mi>W</mi></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi>O</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>186</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>m</mi></math>

Hence, the weight of water that has frozen <math> <mrow> <mtext>=</mtext><mrow><mo>(</mo> <mrow> <mn>250</mn><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>186</mn></mrow> <mo>)</mo></mrow><mi>g</mi><mi>m</mi></mrow> </math>

<math><mo>=</mo><mn>64</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>m</mi></math>

</details>

### Q108. JEE Main 2018 (15 Apr Shift 2 Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

Two 5 molal solutions are prepared by dissolving a non-electrolyte, non-volatile solute separately in the solvents $\mathrm{X}$ and $\mathrm{Y}$. The molecular weights of the solvents are $\mathrm{M}_{\mathrm{X}}$ and $\mathrm{M}_{\mathrm{Y}}$, respectively where $\mathrm{M}_X=\frac{3}{4} \mathrm{M}_{\mathrm{Y}}$. The relative lowering of vapour pressure of the solution in $\mathrm{X}$ is " $\mathrm{m}$ " times that of the solution in Y. Given that the number of moles of solute is very small in comparison to that of solvent, the value of "m" is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{3}{4}$
- **(B)** $\frac{1}{2}$
- **(C)** $\frac{1}{4}$
- **(D)** $\frac{4}{3}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\frac{3}{4}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The relationship between molar masses of the two solvents is
$$
\mathrm{M}_{\mathrm{X}}=\frac{3}{4} \mathrm{M}_{\mathrm{Y}}
$$
The relative lowering of vapour pressure of the two solutions is
$$
\left(\frac{\Delta \mathrm{P}}{\mathrm{P}}\right)_{\mathrm{X}}=\mathrm{m}\left(\frac{\Delta \mathrm{P}}{\mathrm{P}}\right)_{\mathrm{Y}}
$$
But, the relative lowering of vapour pressure of solutions is directly proportional to the mole fraction of solute.
Given 5 molal solution, means 5 moles of solute are dissolved in $1 \mathrm{~kg}$ ( or $1000 \mathrm{~g}$ ) of solvent.
The number of moles of solvent $=\frac{1000 \mathrm{~g}}{\mathrm{M}}$
The mole fraction of solute $=\frac{5}{1000 / \mathrm{M}}$
$$
=\mathrm{M} \times \frac{5}{1000}
$$
hence $\mathrm{M}_{\mathrm{X}} \times \frac{5}{1000}=\mathrm{m} \times \mathrm{M}_{\mathrm{Y}} \times \frac{5}{1000}$.
Substitute equation (i) in equation (ii)
$$
\begin{aligned}
&\frac{3}{4} \times \mathrm{M}_{\mathrm{Y}} \times \frac{5}{1000}=\mathrm{m} \times \mathrm{M}_{\mathrm{Y}} \times \frac{5}{1000} \\
&\mathrm{~m}=\frac{3}{4}
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

### Q109. JEE Main 2018 (08 Apr)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

For<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>molal</mi><mo>&#160;</mo></math>aqueous solution of the following compounds, which one will show the highest freezing point?

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced open="[" close="]" separators="|"><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>3</mn></msub><msub><mi>Cl</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mo>.</mo><mn>3</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced open="[" close="]" separators="|"><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><msub><mi>Cl</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced open="[" close="]" separators="|"><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>5</mn></msub><mi>Cl</mi></mrow></mfenced><msub><mi>Cl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>.</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced open="[" close="]" separators="|"><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>4</mn></msub><msub><mi>Cl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mi>Cl</mi><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced open="[" close="]" separators="|"><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>3</mn></msub><msub><mi>Cl</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mo>.</mo><mn>3</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Depression in freezing point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#916;T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>.</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>

For one molal soloution ; <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#916;T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>&#8733;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></math> is least for <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mo>[</mo><mi>Co</mi><msub><mrow><mo>(</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow><mn>3</mn></msub><msub><mi>Cl</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>]</mo><mo>.</mo><mn>3</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></math> ; So <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#916;T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub></math>  is least but freezing point is high.</p>

</details>

### Q110. JEE Main 2017 (08 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Na</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>SO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></math> was dissolved in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></math> . The change in freezing point was found to be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><msup><mo>.82</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math> . If <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Na</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>SO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>81</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>%</mo></math> ionised, the value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></math>

( <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub></math> for water <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><msup><mo>.86</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi>kg</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> ) is approximately:

(molar mass of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>32</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> and that of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Na</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>23</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> )

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>25</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>65</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>15</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>45</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>45</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Na</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>SO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mo>&#8594;</mo><mn>2</mn><msup><mi>Na</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>+</mo><msubsup><mi>SO</mi><mn>4</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msubsup></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mn>0</mn><mo>.815</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>.63</mo></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mo>.82</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.86</mo><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>.63</mo><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>1000</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>142</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>.86</mo><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>.63</mo><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>5000</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>142</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>.82</mo></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>45</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi>gm</mi></math>

</details>

### Q111. JEE Main 2017 (02 Apr)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>The freezing point of benzene decreases by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mn>0.45</mn><mo>&#176;</mo></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math> on adding <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> of acetic acid to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>20</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> of benzene. If acetic acid associates to form a dimer in benzene, then what is the percentage association of acetic acid in benzene?</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>for</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi>benzene</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>.12</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi>kg</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi></mrow></mfenced></math></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>80</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>%</mo></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>74</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>%</mo></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>94</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>%</mo></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>64</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>%</mo></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>94</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>%</mo></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtable><mtr><mtd><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mi>COOH</mi></mtd><mtd><mo>&#8652;</mo></mtd><mtd><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mi>COOH</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msub></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#945;</mi></mtd><mtd></mtd><mtd><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">&#945;</mi><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mtd></mtr></mtable></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">&#945;</mi><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#916;T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>&#215;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.45</mo><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>.12</mo><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.2</mo><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>1000</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>60</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>20</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.527</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">&#945;</mi><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#945;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.946</mo></math> or <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>94</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>%</mo></math></p>

</details>

### Q112. JEE Main 2016 (10 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

An aqueous solution of a salt <math><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></math> at certain temperature has a Van&#39;t Hoff factor of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn></math>. What is the degree of dissociation for this solution of the salt?

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>50</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>33</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>67</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>80</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>50</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#945;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=0.50</mo></math>

Here,

i= van&#39;t hoff factor

<math> <mi>&#945;</mi> </math> =degree of dissociation

k= total number of ions(dissociated)

</details>

### Q113. JEE Main 2015 (11 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>Determination of the molar mass of acetic acid in benzene using freezing point depression is affected by:</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** Complex formation
- **(B)** Association
- **(C)** Partial ionization
- **(D)** Dissociation

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) Association**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Acetic acid in benzene undergoes dimerization

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mi>COOH</mi><mo>&#8652;</mo><msub><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mi>COOH</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msub></math></p><p>due to which Von&#39;t hoff factor <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mn>1</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mtext>Theoretical&#160;molar&#160;mass</mtext><mtext>Experimental&#160;molar&#160;mass</mtext></mfrac></math></p>

</details>

### Q114. JEE Main 2014 (19 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The observed osmotic pressure for a <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></math> solution of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Fe</mi><mo>(</mo><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>(</mo><msub><mi>SO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>2</mn></msub></math> at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>25</mn><mo>&#176;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>10</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>atm</mi></math>. The expected and experimental (observed) values of Van&#39;t Hoff factor &#39;<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></math>&#39; will be respectively;  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>082</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>atm</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>)</mo></math>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>00</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>42</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>42</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>42</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>42</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math> <mrow> <mi>&#960;</mi> <mo>=</mo> <mtext>i C R T.</mtext> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mn>0</mn> <mtext>.</mtext> <mn>8</mn> <mo>=</mo> <mtext>i</mtext> <mo>&#215;</mo> <mn>0</mn> <mtext>.</mtext> <mn>1</mn> <mn>0</mn> <mo>&#215;</mo> <mn>0</mn> <mtext>.</mtext> <mn>0</mn> <mn>8</mn> <mn>2</mn> <mo>&#215;</mo> <mn>2</mn> <mn>9</mn> <mn>8</mn> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mtext>i</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mn>0</mn> <mtext>.</mtext> <mn>8</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>0</mn> <mtext>.</mtext> <mn>1</mn> <mn>0</mn> <mo>&#215;</mo> <mn>0</mn> <mtext>.</mtext> <mn>0</mn> <mn>8</mn> <mn>2</mn> <mo>&#215;</mo> <mn>2</mn> <mn>9</mn> <mn>8</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mn>0</mn> <mtext>.</mtext> <mn>8</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mo>.</mo> <mn>4</mn> <mn>4</mn> <mn>3</mn> <mn>6</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math> <math> <mrow> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mn>0</mn> <mtext>.</mtext> <mn>8</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mtext>.</mtext> <mn>4</mn> <mn>4</mn> <mn>3</mn> <mn>8</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>42</mn></math> (experimental value)

<math> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>FeSO</mtext> <mn>4</mn> </msub> <mtext>.</mtext> <munder> <mrow> <msub> <mrow> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>NH</mtext> <mn>4</mn> </msub> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msub> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>0</mn> <mo>&#183;</mo> <mn>1</mn> <mtext>M</mtext> </mrow> </munder> <msub> <mtext>SO</mtext> <mn>4</mn> </msub> <mo>&#10230;</mo> <munder> <mrow> <msubsup> <mtext>Fe</mtext> <mtext>aq</mtext> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mo>+</mo> </mrow> </msubsup> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>0</mn> <mn>1</mn> <mtext>M</mtext> </mrow> </munder> <mo>+</mo> <mn>2</mn> <munder> <mrow> <msubsup> <mtext>SO</mtext> <mrow> <mn>4</mn> <mtext>aq</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mo>-</mo> </mrow> </msubsup> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>0</mn> <mo>&#183;</mo> <mn>2</mn> <mtext>M</mtext> </mrow> </munder> <mo>+</mo> <mn>2</mn> <munder> <mrow> <msubsup> <mtext>NH</mtext> <mrow> <mn>4</mn> <mtext>aq</mtext> </mrow> <mo>+</mo> </msubsup> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>0</mn> <mo>&#183;</mo> <mn>2</mn> <mtext>M</mtext> </mrow> </munder> </mrow> </math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></math> (expected)<math> <mrow> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>0</mn> <mo>&#183;</mo> <mn>5</mn> <mtext>M</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>0</mn> <mo>&#183;</mo> <mn>1</mn> <mtext>M</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mn>5</mn> </mrow> </math>

</details>

### Q115. JEE Main 2014 (06 Apr)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

Consider separate solutions of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>500</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>5</mn></msub><mi>OH</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mi>aq</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>100</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>Mg</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>PO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mi>aq</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>250</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>KBr</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mi>aq</mi></mfenced></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>125</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>Na</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><msub><mi>PO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mi>aq</mi></mfenced></math> at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>25</mn><mo>&#176;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math>. Which statement is true about these solutions, assuming all salts to be strong electrolytes?

**Options:**

- **(A)** They all have the same osmotic pressure.
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>100</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>Mg</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>(</mo><msub><mi>PO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mi>aq</mi></mfenced></math> has the highest osmotic pressure.
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>125</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>Na</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><msub><mi>PO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mi>aq</mi></mfenced></math> has the highest osmotic pressure.
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>500</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>5</mn></msub><mi>OH</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mi>aq</mi></mfenced></math> has the highest osmotic pressure.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) They all have the same osmotic pressure.**

**Detailed Solution:**

Osmotic pressure  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mo>=</mo><mtext>iCRT</mtext></math>(i is  Van&#39;t  Hoff factor, C is concentration)

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>iC</mi></math> at same temperature.

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>5</mn></msub><mi>OH</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mspace linebreak="newline"/><msub><mi>Mg</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>(</mo><msub><mi>PO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mspace linebreak="newline"/><mi>KBr</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>25</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mspace linebreak="newline"/><msub><mi>Na</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><msub><mi>PO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>125</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn></math>

The solutions are isotonic as they have the same osmotic pressure.

</details>

### Q116. JEE Main 2013 (22 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

A molecule $M$ associates in a given solvent according to the equation $\mathrm{M} \rightleftharpoons(\mathrm{M})_n$. For a certain concentration of M, the van't Hoff factor was found to be $0.9$ and the fraction of associated molecules was $0.2$. The value of $n$ is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** 3
- **(B)** 5
- **(C)** 2
- **(D)** 4

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) 2**

**Detailed Solution:**

van't Hoff factor (i) and the degree of association are related as below :
$$
\begin{aligned}
& i=1-\alpha\left(1-\frac{1}{n}\right) \\
& 0.9=1-0.2\left(1-\frac{1}{n}\right)
\end{aligned}
$$
On solving,
$$
\begin{aligned}
& \left(1-\frac{1}{n}\right)=\frac{1}{2} \\
& \frac{1}{n}=1-\frac{1}{2}=\frac{1}{2} \\
& \therefore n=2
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

### Q117. JEE Main 2013 (09 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

$12 \mathrm{~g}$ of a nonvolatile solute dissolved in $108 \mathrm{~g}$ of water produces the relative lowering of vapour pressure of $0.1$. The molecular mass of the solute is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** $80$
- **(B)** $60$
- **(C)** $20$
- **(D)** $40$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $20$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\frac{\mathrm{P}^{\mathrm{o}}-\mathrm{P}_s}{\mathrm{P}^{\mathrm{o}}}=\frac{n}{N}=\frac{w}{m} \times \frac{\mathrm{M}}{W}$
$0.1=\frac{12}{m} \times \frac{18}{108}$
$m=\frac{12 \times 18}{0.1 \times 108}=20$

</details>

### Q118. JEE Main 2012 (26 May Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The freezing point of a $1.00 \mathrm{~m}$ aqueous solution of $\mathrm{HF}$ is found to be $-1.91^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$. The freezing point constant of water, $K_f$ is $1.86 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$. The percentage dissociation of $\mathrm{HF}$ at this concentration is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $30 \%$
- **(B)** $10 \%$
- **(C)** $5.2 \%$
- **(D)** $2.7 \%$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $2.7 \%$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\Delta T_{\mathrm{f}}=K_{\mathrm{f}} \times m \times i$
$$
\begin{aligned}
& i=\frac{\Delta T_{\mathrm{f}}}{K_{\mathrm{f}} \times m}=\frac{1.91}{1.86 \times 1}=1.02 \\
& \text { For } \mathrm{HI} \leftrightharpoons \mathrm{H}^{+}+\mathrm{I}^{-} \\
& \quad(1-\alpha) \quad \alpha \quad \alpha \\
& 1-\alpha+\alpha+\alpha=i=1.027 \\
& 1+\alpha=1.027 \\
& \alpha=0.027 \text { or } 2.7 \%
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

---

### Q119. JEE Main 2012 (12 May Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

A solution containing $0.85 \mathrm{~g}$ of $\mathrm{ZnCl}_2$ in $125.0 \mathrm{~g}$ of water freezes at $-0.23^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$. The apparent degree of dissociation of the salt is $\left(K_f\right.$ for water $=1.86 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$, atomic mass: $\mathrm{Zn}=65.3$ and $\mathrm{Cl}=35.5)$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $1.36 \%$
- **(B)** $73.5 \%$
- **(C)** $7.35 \%$
- **(D)** $2.47 \%$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $73.5 \%$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$$
\text { Mol.wt. }=\frac{k_f \times w \times 1000}{\Delta T_f \times W}=\frac{1.86 \times 0.85 \times 1000}{0.23 \times 125} \approx 55 \mathrm{gm}
$$
Where
$$
\begin{aligned}
& w=0.85 \mathrm{~g} \\
& W=125 \mathrm{~g} \\
& \Delta T_f=0^{\circ} \mathrm{C}-\left(-23^{\circ} \mathrm{C}\right)=23^{\circ} \mathrm{C}
\end{aligned}
$$
Now, $i=\frac{M_{\text {normal }}}{M_{\text {observed }}}=\frac{136.3}{55}=2.47$
$$
\begin{aligned}
& \underset{1-\alpha}{\mathrm{ZnCl}_2} \leftrightharpoons \underset{\alpha}{\mathrm{Zn}^{++}}+\underset{2 \alpha}{2 \mathrm{Cl}^{-}} \\
& \text {Van't Hoff factor ( } i \text { ) } \\
& =\frac{1-\alpha+\alpha+2 \alpha}{1}=2.47 \\
& \therefore \quad \alpha=0.735=73.5 \% \\
&
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

### Q120. JEE Main 2012 (Offline)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

$\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{f}}$ for water is $1.86 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$. If your automobile radiator holds $1.0 \mathrm{~kg}$ of water, how many grams of ethylene glycol $\left(\mathrm{C}_2 \mathrm{H}_6 \mathrm{O}_2\right)$ must you add to get the freezing point of the solution lowered to $-2.8^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$ ?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $72 \mathrm{~g}$
- **(B)** $93 \mathrm{~g}$
- **(C)** $39 \mathrm{~g}$
- **(D)** $27 \mathrm{~g}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $93 \mathrm{~g}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{f}}=\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{f}} \cdot \mathrm{m}$
$2.8=1.86 \times \frac{\mathrm{wt}}{62} \times \frac{1000}{1000}$
$\mathrm{Wt}=93 \mathrm{~g}$

</details>

### Q121. JEE Main 2011

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Ethylene glycol is used as an antifreeze in a cold climate. Mass of ethylene glycol which should be added to $4 \mathrm{~kg}$ of water to prevent it from freezing at $-6^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$ will be :
$\left[\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{t}}\right.$ for water $=1.86 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$, and molar mass of ethylene glycol $=62 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$ )

**Options:**

- **(A)** $204.30 \mathrm{~g}$
- **(B)** $400.00 \mathrm{~g}$
- **(C)** $304.60 \mathrm{~g}$
- **(D)** $804.32 \mathrm{~g}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $804.32 \mathrm{~g}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$$
\Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{f}}=\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{f}} \times \mathrm{m}=\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{f}} \times \frac{\mathrm{w}_2 \times 1000}{\mathrm{w}_1 \times \mathrm{m}_2}
$$
$w_1 \& w_2=$ wt of solvent \& solute respecting $m_2=m w$ of solute
$$
\Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{f}}=0^{\circ}-\left(-6^{\circ}\right)=6=1.86 \times \frac{\mathrm{w}_2 \times 1000}{4000 \times 62}
$$
Therefore $w_2=800 \mathrm{~g}$

</details>

### Q122. JEE Main 2011

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

The degree of dissociation $(\alpha)$ of a weak electrolyte, $A_x B_y$ is related to van't Hoff factor (i) by the expression:

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\alpha=\frac{i-1}{x+y+1}$
- **(B)** $\alpha=\frac{x+y-1}{i-1}$
- **(C)** $\alpha=\frac{x+y+1}{i-1}$
- **(D)** $\alpha=\frac{i-1}{(x+y-1)}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\alpha=\frac{i-1}{(x+y-1)}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$$
\begin{aligned}
& i=1-\alpha+n \alpha=1+\alpha(n-1) \\
& \frac{i-1}{n-1}=\alpha \\
& A_x B_y \rightarrow x A^{+y}+y B^{-x} \\
& n=x+y
\end{aligned}
$$
So $\alpha=\frac{i-1}{x+y-1}$

</details>

### Q123. JEE Main 2010

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

If sodium sulphate is considered to be completely dissociated into cations and anions in aqueous solution, the change in freezing point of water $\left(\Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{f}}\right)$, when $0.01 \mathrm{~mol}$ of sodium sulphate is dissolved in $1 \mathrm{~kg}$ of water, is $\left(\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{f}}=1.86 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}\right)$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $0.0372 \mathrm{~K}$
- **(B)** $0.0558 \mathrm{~K}$
- **(C)** $0.0744 \mathrm{~K}$
- **(D)** $0.0186 \mathrm{~K}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $0.0558 \mathrm{~K}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Vant Hoff's factor (i) for $\mathrm{Na}_2 \mathrm{SO}_4=3$
$$
\begin{aligned}
\therefore \Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{f}}= & \text { (i) } \mathrm{k}_{\mathrm{f}} \mathrm{m} \\
& =3 \times 1.80 \times \frac{0.01}{1}=0.0558 \mathrm{~K}
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

### Q126. JEE Main 2002

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Freezing point of an aqueous solution is $(-0.186)^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$. Elevation of boiling point of the same solution is $\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{b}}=0.512^{\circ} \mathrm{C}, \mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{f}}=1.86^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$, find the increase in boiling point.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $0.186{ }^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$
- **(B)** $0.0512{ }^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$
- **(C)** $0.092{ }^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$
- **(D)** $0.2372{ }^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $0.0512{ }^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{b}}=\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{b}} \times \frac{\mathrm{W}_{\mathrm{B}}}{\mathrm{M}_{\mathrm{B}} \times \mathrm{W}_{\mathrm{A}}} \times 1000 ; \Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{f}}=\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{f}} \frac{\mathrm{W}_{\mathrm{B}}}{\mathrm{M}_{\mathrm{B}} \times \mathrm{W}_{\mathrm{A}}} \times 1000 ; \frac{\Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{b}}}{\Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{f}}}=\frac{\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{b}}}{\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{f}}}=\frac{\Delta \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{b}}}{-0.186}=\frac{0.512}{1.86}=0.0512^0 \mathrm{C}$

</details>
